- 2026-09-07: **Product search is now hybrid (full-text + semantic vector) and server-ranked, and every fabricated data source on the two search surfaces is gone.** Eight new migrations (`20260906120000`–`20260906190000`, all **applied**); new edge functions `generate-embedding` and `embed-query` (both **deployed**, `verify_jwt = true`); new `src/lib/queries/search.ts` and `scripts/search-smoke.mjs`; `src/lib/queries/products.ts`, `src/pages/Search.tsx`, `src/pages/SearchResults.tsx`, `src/pages/ProductDetail.tsx`, `src/lib/database.types.ts`, `supabase/config.toml`.
  - **The architectural point, in one sentence:** ranking used to be a JS `.includes()` in the browser over the entire live table, with plan boost as a *separate* client-side sort key — so there was no way to express "a highly relevant free listing should outrank a marginally relevant paid one". `match_products` now fuses keyword rank and vector rank with **RRF (k=60, untuned)** and applies boost as one visible multiplication *after* fusion.
  - **Two parts of the original spec were impossible as written, both caught before applying anything.** (1) `fts tsvector generated always as (to_tsvector('english', search_text))` is rejected outright — *"A generated column cannot reference another generated column"* — so `fts` inlines the full expression; both still derive from the same own-row columns and cannot drift. (2) **`array_to_string` is STABLE, not IMMUTABLE**, so `pattern`/`occasion` could not appear in a generated column at all; a narrow `immutable_array_to_string(text[], text)` wrapper is the fix, and it is genuinely immutable for `text[]` with a literal separator. Both were verified by attempting them against a temp table first rather than discovering them in a production migration.
  - **`category_name` is denormalised onto `products`** and kept in sync by a BEFORE `UPDATE OF category_id` trigger, so `search_text`/`fts` stay pure generated columns off own-row data. This exists because **category is the best-populated field in the table (29/29) and a generated column cannot run a subquery.** It is already earning its place: a search for `t-shirt` matches "Ribbed Tank Top" *only* through its `T-shirts/Tops` category.
  - **Category renames cascade, and the subtle part was verified rather than assumed.** An AFTER `UPDATE OF name` trigger on `categories` issues `UPDATE products SET category_id = category_id`. That looks like a no-op and is not: `UPDATE ... OF <col>` fires on the columns **named as a target**, not on the ones whose values changed — confirmed against this database, the no-op write produced a correct queue job. It is SECURITY DEFINER because the admin renaming a category is not necessarily a `product_moderator`, and `products`' UPDATE policy would otherwise reject the cascade. Both existing BEFORE triggers are safe against it (`enforce_product_cap`'s `adds_slot` is false when status is unchanged; `enforce_products_moderation` returns early when neither status nor rejection_reason moved).
  - **Data reality that shaped the design, measured before writing any of it:** of 29 live products, `category_id` 29, `fabric` 26, `fit_type` 26, `colour` 26, `gsm` 15, `description` 9 — and `pattern`, `occasion`, `neck_type`, `sleeve_type`, `collar_type` are **0/29, empty on every single row**. They are still in `search_text` for when vendors fill them, but they contribute nothing today; the real embedding input is name + fabric + gsm + fit + colour + category.
  - **Three junk rows were moved `live` → `draft`** (name `Abhishek Mitra`, description `Jshshshs`, no attributes, same vendor, created 2026-07-10). They would otherwise have been embedded and surfaced as neighbours. Live count 29 → 26. Owner-approved, reversible.
  - **The embedding pipeline is self-healing and currently idle by design.** `pgmq` queue + `pg_cron` + `pg_net` → `generate-embedding`, which archives a message **only after** the row is written; any failure leaves it queued for the next tick. **OPENAI_API_KEY is set but the OpenAI account has no billing yet**, so the backfill has deliberately not been enqueued — when billing is enabled the jobs drain with no operator action.
  - **Two deliberate departures from the spec on the cron, both cost-driven.** Poll **every minute, not every 10 s** (a 10 s poll spends ~260k of the 500k free monthly edge invocations doing nothing, and products sit in 24–48 h moderation anyway, so sub-minute lag is unobservable); and the HTTP call **only fires when a visible message is actually waiting**, so an idle marketplace costs zero invocations.
  - **Security notes worth keeping.** `generate-embedding` requires `role = 'service_role'` *inside* the handler on top of the platform gate — verified: the public anon key gets **403 forbidden**, closing an "anyone with the bundled key can burn OpenAI credits" hole. `embed-query` is the buyer-facing one and is deliberately a **separate function**, so an auth mistake on the public path cannot expose the queue drainer. Query vectors never reach the browser: `search_products` resolves the cached embedding server-side (an earlier shape shipped 1536 floats each way for a value the browser cannot use). `status = 'live'` inside the SECURITY DEFINER RPCs is load-bearing — it is the only thing keeping drafts out of results, and it was tested (0 junk rows leak).
  - **`pg_net` registers against `public` but its functions live under `net`** — the cron poller must call `net.http_post`. Recorded because it is not guessable. Likewise, a SECURITY DEFINER function pinned to `search_path = public` **cannot resolve the `<=>` operator at all**; `extensions` must be on the path.
  - **Fabricated data removed** (this is the bulk of the frontend diff): `SEARCH_SUGGESTIONS` (invented volumes like "Handbags For Clothes 2,348,215"), `BRAND_SUGGESTIONS` (**H&M / Zara / Levi's** — real trademarked brands that are not vendors here, with invented follower counts and ids routing to vendor pages that do not exist), `POPULAR_KEYWORDS` (hardcoded list under a heading stamped with *today's date*, implying a trending job that was never built), `TRENDING_KEYWORDS`, `RECOMMENDED_PRODUCTS` (15 picsum listings shown whenever the real catalogue was empty — a direct violation of this project's own "No mock data in production" rule), `BASE_PRODUCTS`, `FOR_HIM_CATEGORIES`, the "Father's Day gifting top picks" seasonal claim, a `Sponsored` label over non-sponsored results, `BRAND_RESULTS` (six invented manufacturers with invented ratings/reviews/"9,999+" followers) and `BRAND_PICKS` (a "sponsored" rail of six fake listings priced in **USD on an INR marketplace**, with a Call Now button wired to nothing).
  - **What replaced them is counted from real rows:** autocomplete via `search_suggestions` (categories with real live-listing counts, real product names with real enquiry counts, real vendor storefronts with real follower counts from `follows`); the category rail is grouped off the live catalogue the page already fetched (no extra request); the Brand tab is grouped out of the ranked product results, so it can never disagree with the Product tab beside it. Rating/reviews/followers are **not** shown on brand cards — they exist in the schema but not on the rows that tab already has, and inventing them is what got the previous version into trouble.
  - **`useYouMightLike` changed meaning, deliberately.** It ranked by the *buyer's* saved category preferences, which answered "things you generally like" rather than "things like this", and could only resolve to a whole category bucket. It is now nearest-neighbour on the product embedding via `related_products`, with a same-category fallback for anchors with no embedding. Buyer preference still drives the home feed (`filterForYou`). `resolvePreferredCategoryIds` and `fetchProductsByCategoryIds` became dead and were deleted.
  - **Loading / empty / results are now three distinct states** on SearchResults (skeletons, an honest empty state with a Post-a-requirement CTA, and results) — previously a failed fetch rendered eight fake products and passed for a populated catalogue. The result footer reads `N results` and appends **"· keyword match only"** whenever the query had no cached embedding, so a degraded search says so.
  - **The "Scroll for more" infinite scroll was removed**: it only ever grew the *fake* placeholder set — the real catalogue was always rendered in full on first paint — so it advertised pagination this page has never had.
  - **Verified:** `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json` holds at the unchanged **23-error baseline** (all in files not touched here); eslint clean on every changed file; `vite build` green; and `scripts/search-smoke.mjs` **10/10** against a real dev server, including "no fabricated data" sweeps on both surfaces, real autocomplete counts, 7 real results for `shirt`, a real Brand tab, an honest empty state, and **zero console errors**. `related_products` verified on ProductDetail (8 links, fallback branch, no errors).
  - **A security-advisor pass after the DDL caught two real misses, both mine.** `get_advisors(security)` went **76 → 66** (migration `20260906200000`). Postgres grants EXECUTE to PUBLIC by default, so the three new **trigger** functions were reachable at `/rest/v1/rpc/<name>` by anon — harmless in practice (a trigger function called directly errors) but this repo already revokes exactly this (20260801100327, 20260905182854). And `immutable_array_to_string` / `normalise_search_query` had role-mutable search_paths, now pinned — checked first that pinning is accepted despite a generated column depending on one of them. `match_products` was also revoked from anon/authenticated: it is the ranking engine, not the entry point, and being reachable let anyone POST an arbitrary 1536-float vector. Verified through the real REST path with the anon key — `search_products` still works, `match_products` answers `42501`, and the smoke test is still 10/10. Everything still flagged is intentional and written down: the three buyer-facing RPCs must be anon-callable, `search_query_embeddings` is deliberately RLS-on-with-no-policies, and `extension_in_public: pg_net` is cosmetic (all 12 of its functions are in `net`, none in `public`, confirmed via `pg_depend`).
  - **Open, and needed from the owner:** (1) `select vault.create_secret('<service_role key>', 'service_role_key', …)` in the dashboard SQL editor — deliberately not in a migration so the key never enters git; (2) OpenAI billing, after which Phase 2 backfill + Phase 5 relevance verification can run. Also note **every live product currently resolves to `boost_tier = 0`** — `Demo Textiles Co.` (gold, 6 live listings) expired 2026-08-16 and the only active plan has no live listings — so the boost path is correct and tested but not observable on today's data.
- 2026-09-06: **Phase 8 — the browser half of the fixture test now exists and is green, closing the last open item. The "it's blocked on re-seeding fixture accounts" claim in the entry below was wrong, and no re-seed was needed.** New `tests/video-closeups-bunny.spec.ts` (6 cases, T8.1–T8.5).
  - **CORRECTION: the Playwright half was never blocked.** The entry below reported it could not be written until `seed-chat-fixtures.sql` / `seed-test-admins.sql` were re-run, and framed that as a decision about putting known-password logins back into a live database. That conflated *the chat suite's* fixtures with *any* browser test. `chat-pipeline.spec.ts`'s "FIXTURES ONLY, never demo-*" rule states its own reason — `messages` has no DELETE policy for any role, so every message a test sends is permanent — and that reason does not transfer to video: `product_videos` has `pvideos_delete`, and `scripts/bunny-e2e-check.mjs` had already been round-tripping insert → delete against `demo-vendor` for two sessions. `demo-vendor` / `demo-admin` (`super_admin`, active) / `demo-buyer` all exist and sign in. Nothing needed seeding; a decision was put to the owner that did not need making.
  - **What the browser proves that the API script structurally cannot.** Bunny's "block direct URL file access" refuses any request with a blank `Referer`, and Node's `fetch` sends none — so from a script every playback URL is 403 whether the file is there or not. That is exactly how the earlier session mistook working hotlink protection for a dead pull zone. A real browser loading a real `<video>` sends a real `Referer`, so **T8.3 and T8.5 assert `readyState >= 2` (a frame actually decoded) and `videoWidth > 0`** against the stored Bunny MP4 — in the moderator's player and in the buyer reel. That is the retraction measured rather than argued: the pull zone serves fine, from both apps.
  - **T8.1 asserts the claim the brief actually makes**, which is not "an error appears" but "rejected *before any upload attempt*". A `page.on("request")` spy records anything matching `video.bunnycdn.com`, `/storage/v1/upload/resumable` or `/functions/v1/bunny-upload-url`, and the assertion is that the list is empty. A toast alone would have passed against a gate that fires the message and uploads anyway.
  - **T8.1b is the negative control's opposite number.** The same bytes with their real MP4 brand must be *accepted*. Without it, T8.1 passes just as well against a gate that rejects everything — the failure mode this suite already tripped over once, when the blank-`Referer` 403 was miscoded as a failure.
  - **The `.mov` fixture is derived from real video, not stubbed.** ffmpeg is not on PATH here, so rather than fabricate 12 bytes, the spec reads the real 478×850 vendor clip, verifies `ftyp` at bytes 4–8, and rewrites only the 4-byte major brand to `qt  ` — a QuickTime signature on top of genuine video data, which is precisely and entirely what `sniffVideoContainer` inspects. The spec's own comment records the boundary: this proves the gate discriminates on container signature, and claims nothing about HEVC or about Bunny's behaviour on such a file (out of scope by instruction).
  - **Strict mode caught a real hazard, not a selector nit.** `getByRole("button", {name: "Approve"})` also matches **"Approve all for vendor…"** — `approve_vendor_content_bulk`, which approves that vendor's pending *products and catalogues* too. Playwright refused the ambiguous click; an auto-first-match API would have silently taken one or the other. Both the click and its card filter are now `exact: true`, with the reason written down.
  - **T8.4 asserts the UI and the database separately.** The row leaving the queue is the UI's report; `product_videos.status = 'live'` read back independently is the truth. Those two disagreeing is the entire reason this layer exists — it is the `vendor_profiles.account_status` regression's shape, a perfect DB under a UI that had not caught up.
  - **Green: 6/6 in 48s.** Cleanup is in `afterAll` and provider-first (asset at Bunny, then the row — the same order `deleteProductVideo()` uses, so a crash cannot strand a paid asset). Verified after the run: Bunny library **0 videos / 0 orphans**, and `product_videos` back to the single real `provider='supabase'` row (`Yoyoyo`, rejected), untouched. It also held through the *failed* intermediate run, which is the harder case.
  - **`test-results/` was tracked in git and is now ignored.** Two Playwright artifacts had been committed previously; this spec stages a ~4 MB video fixture in that directory, so the next commit would have put a binary in the repo. Added `test-results/` and `playwright-report/` to `.gitignore` and `git rm --cached` on the two stray files (they stay on disk). Durable evidence for `test.md` belongs in `screenshots/`, which Playwright does not wipe.
  - `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json` holds at the unchanged 23-error baseline (`tests/` is outside that project; the spec typechecks clean standalone).
- 2026-09-06: **Phase 8 — Bunny Stream now passes end to end against the real library. Both blockers reported yesterday are resolved, and one of them was my own measurement error.** `supabase/functions/bunny-upload-url/index.ts` (deployed v3), `src/lib/queries/videos.ts`, `scripts/bunny-e2e-check.mjs`, `documentation/claude.md`, `documentation/technicalimplementation.md`, `documentation/test.md`.
  - **RETRACTION: the "pull zone 403s everything" blocker was not real.** Yesterday's entry reported that `play_480p.mp4`, `thumbnail.jpg` and `playlist.m3u8` all returned 403 and concluded playback was blocked catalogue-wide. That conclusion was wrong, and the fault was in the probe, not the CDN. Bunny's documented causes of a Stream 403 include **"block direct URL file access", which rejects any request with a blank `Referer`** — and a script `HEAD` sends none, while a browser playing a `<video>` always does. Re-probed with a Referer: `http://localhost:8080/` → **200**, `https://textile-spark-net.vercel.app/` → **200**, for the MP4 rendition, the thumbnail and the HLS manifest alike. Real playback works. The lesson is recorded in `claude.md` rather than just fixed, because anything that probes these URLs in future will hit the same trap: **hotlink protection is ON deliberately and must not be "fixed"** — but a new origin (a custom domain) does have to be added to Bunny's referrer allowlist or playback stops there.
  - **The rendition blocker was real, and is now fixed and tested.** The brief locked "store the play_720p.mp4 rendition URL". Measured: the platform's own vendor clip (478×850 portrait phone video) encodes to **240p, 360p and 480p and no 720p**, because Bunny only builds renditions the source supports — so a hardcoded 720p URL 404s for precisely the content this marketplace receives. The locked decision's *intent* (serve the MP4 fallback rendition, not HLS) is unchanged; only the resolution label is now derived. `pickRendition()` picks from the shorter edge of the client-probed dimensions with a small tolerance — 478 selects 480p, which is what Bunny actually built, where a strict `<=` would have selected 360p and served a worse file than existed. A ≥720p source still gets 720p. 1080p is deliberately off the ladder: this is a 9:16 phone reel and Bunny egress is billed.
  - **`UploadVideo.tsx` already had the numbers.** `probeVideoFile()` has always measured width/height for the layout-shift fix; they are now also sent to `bunny-upload-url`, so nothing new is computed on the client. Missing or nonsense dimensions fall back to the 720p cap, which is the prior behaviour and correct for any source large enough to matter.
  - **The e2e check now asserts the choice, not just the outcome:** "the rendition the function chose must be one Bunny actually produced". That is what turns `pickRendition()` from a heuristic into a tested rule, and it fails loudly if Bunny's ladder ever changes.
  - **A negative control was miscoded as a failure and is now correct.** The blank-`Referer` 403 is hotlink protection *working*; asserting 200 there kept the suite permanently red for correct behaviour, which is how a suite gets ignored. It now asserts the pair — blocked when hotlink-shaped, served from real browser origins — and the run is green.
  - **Full green run (20 assertions):** slot minted with no API key in the response; TUS upload; encoding finished; MP4 Fallback confirmed enabled on the library; renditions produced; **chosen rendition exists at Bunny**; hotlink protection blocks a blank referer; dev and production origins both served; thumbnail served; **the moderation trigger still forces `under_review` on a `provider='bunny'` insert that explicitly asked for `live`**; `provider` + `bunny_video_id` persist; `bunny-delete-video` removes the asset, confirmed by the GUID leaving the library listing via Bunny's API; throwaway row cleaned up. Library left at **0 videos, 0 orphans**; `product_videos` back to the single real `provider='supabase'` row, untouched and un-backfilled exactly as the brief requires.
  - **Deployment drift closed.** `bunny-upload-url` is now v3 and carries the six-hour signature window the repo has (v1 shipped with one hour, which would have dropped a 50 MB upload on a slow connection at ~95%). All three functions are deployed with `verify_jwt = true`, matching their `config.toml` stanzas — the manual JWT decode inside each is only sound because of that.
  - **Still not done:** the Playwright half of the fixture test. ~~The suite it would join is unrunnable until `seed-chat-fixtures.sql` / `seed-test-admins.sql` are re-run, which is a decision about putting known-password logins back into a live database.~~ **[SUPERSEDED same day — that was wrong; no re-seed was ever needed. See the entry above.]** What remains browser-side is the renamed-`.mov` client rejection and the moderation-queue / buyer-feed rendering; the API layer — upload, encode, rendition choice, moderation gating, deletion — is now covered by `scripts/bunny-e2e-check.mjs`.
- 2026-09-06: **Phase 8 — the Bunny chain is built, deployed and proven end to end against the real library; go-live is blocked on two Bunny DASHBOARD settings, both now measured rather than guessed.** New `scripts/bunny-e2e-check.mjs`; all three edge functions deployed; `supabase/functions/bunny-reconcile/index.ts`, `supabase/functions/bunny-delete-video/index.ts`, `documentation/claude.md`, `documentation/orphan-reconciliation.sql`, `documentation/test.md`, `documentation/technicalimplementation.md`.
  - **The secrets are set now.** `scripts/bunny-config-check.mjs` reports `CONFIGURED`, `cdnHostname=vz-835c6bf5-df7.b-cdn.net`. Last session all three were absent; this closes that blocker. The probe branch still creates no Bunny video, which is what makes it safe to run repeatedly.
  - **What is proven working, by a real upload of the platform's own 478×850 vendor clip:** `bunny-upload-url` mints a slot and its response carries no API key; TUS uploads straight to Bunny; encoding completes; the row inserts with `provider='bunny'` + `bunny_video_id`; **the moderation trigger still forces `under_review` even when the insert explicitly asks for `live`** — which was the single most important thing to confirm, since the whole premise is that changing storage provider changes nothing about moderation; `bunny-delete-video` really removes the asset, confirmed by the GUID disappearing from the library listing; and the library is left at **0 videos, 0 orphans** afterwards.
  - **BLOCKER 1 — the pull zone 403s everything.** Not the composed URL only: `play_480p.mp4` (which Bunny's API confirms exists), `thumbnail.jpg` and `playlist.m3u8` all return 403. So this is a pull-zone/library setting — Token Authentication, a referrer/geo rule, or an embed-view-token requirement — and it blocks MP4 and HLS **equally**, meaning it cannot be worked around by changing the URL format or by adopting HLS earlier. Nothing can play until it is changed in the Bunny dashboard.
  - **BLOCKER 2 — `play_720p.mp4` does not exist for real content, and now we know it rather than suspect it.** The locked decision was to store the 720p rendition URL. Uploading the platform's actual vendor video (478×850 portrait, a phone clip) produced **240p, 360p and 480p and no 720p**: Bunny only generates renditions the source supports. A hardcoded `play_720p.mp4` therefore 404s for precisely the content this marketplace receives. This was flagged as a risk when the phase was scoped, reaffirmed as locked, and has now been measured — so it is recorded as evidence, not as a re-opened argument. **No guess was shipped:** the composition is unchanged, and `bunny-e2e-check.mjs` hard-fails on it so it cannot go live unnoticed. Choosing between "predict the rendition from the probed source dimensions" and "insert first, PATCH `video_url` from `availableResolutions` after encoding" is a design call for the owner; the second is robust, the first is a heuristic (note Bunny produced 480p from a 478-wide source, so the ladder is not a clean `<=` on the short edge).
  - **The oracle had to change, and that is itself a finding.** The first version of the e2e script probed the public CDN with HEAD. On a protected pull zone that is worthless twice over: it cannot distinguish "rendition missing" from "rendition present but blocked", and its delete assertion **passed for the wrong reason** — 403 before the delete and 403 after. Both answers now come from `bunny-reconcile`, which reads the library through the API with the key that never leaves the edge runtime. The phase brief had specified exactly this ("verify via a follow-up list/get call against Bunny's API, not just that the DB row is gone"); the CDN shortcut looked equivalent and was not.
  - **`bunny-reconcile` gained a `library` block** carrying `status`, `encodeProgress`, `availableResolutions`, `hasMP4Fallback` and `thumbnailFileName` per video. That is what makes it usable as the "did this encode, and into what" oracle — questions the CDN cannot answer on a protected zone. It stays read-only and admin-gated.
  - **All three functions are deployed with `verify_jwt = true`**, matching their `config.toml` stanzas — the manual JWT decode inside each is only sound because of that. `bunny-upload-url` is still at **version 1, carrying the one-hour signature window**; the repo has six hours. That gap matters (tus resends the auth headers on every PATCH, so the expiry bounds the whole upload, and 50 MB on a slow connection can exceed an hour) but it is not urgent while playback is 403, and the file must change again anyway once the rendition question above is settled. **Deploy it with the other two at go-live, per step 3 of the checklist in `claude.md`.**
  - **Also fixed:** `bunny-e2e-check.mjs` initially crashed resolving its own temp path, because this repo lives under a directory containing a space and `new URL(...).pathname` hands that back percent-encoded — `fileURLToPath` is the fix, worth knowing for any future script here.
  - **Still not done:** the Playwright half of the fixture test. The suite it would join is unrunnable — the `chatfx-*` / `rlstest-*` fixture accounts were deleted in an earlier session and `seed-chat-fixtures.sql` / `seed-test-admins.sql` have not been re-run, which is a decision about putting known-password logins back into a live database. The API-layer half now exists and covers upload, encode, moderation gating and deletion; what remains browser-side is the renamed-`.mov` client rejection and the moderation-queue/buyer-feed rendering, and the latter cannot be asserted while the CDN 403s.
- 2026-09-06: **Phase 8 — Bunny Stream migration: everything that does not depend on the credentials is built and verified; the go-live is BLOCKED because the credentials are not on the project.** New `supabase/functions/bunny-upload-url/`, `bunny-delete-video/`, `bunny-reconcile/`; new migration `20260905214044_product_videos_bunny_video_id.sql` (**applied**); new `scripts/bunny-config-check.mjs`; `src/lib/queries/videos.ts`, `src/components/buyer/VideoCloseUpsViewer.tsx`, `src/pages/UploadVideo.tsx`, `src/lib/database.types.ts`, `supabase/config.toml`, `documentation/orphan-reconciliation.sql`, `documentation/claude.md`. In Cosora-Admin: `src/pages/Videos.tsx`.
  - **The blocker, stated plainly.** The brief said BUNNY_API_KEY and BUNNY_LIBRARY_ID were already stored as edge-function secrets on `vxdhhgdfubqedfpwfyrb`. **They are not set.** `supabase secrets list` could not be used (no management access token in this environment, and none in either repo's `.env` by design), so the question was answered from the other end: `bunny-upload-url` was deployed and called with its `{"probe":true}` branch as a real signed-in vendor. It answered `missing secret(s): BUNNY_API_KEY, BUNNY_LIBRARY_ID, BUNNY_CDN_HOSTNAME` — twice, on two separate runs, against a function deployed minutes earlier (so this is absence, not propagation lag). Per the brief's own instruction, no placeholder was invented and no key was requested in plaintext. **Nothing is broken by this**: `createProductVideo` falls back to the existing Supabase Storage path, so uploads work exactly as before and every new row is still `provider='supabase'`.
  - **`{"probe":true}` exists precisely because of that question.** It reports the configuration verdict WITHOUT creating a Bunny video object — the only other way to check is to run a real upload, which leaves a stray empty video in the library every single time anyone checks. It sits behind the function's FULL authorization gate (vendor + active account), not merely behind authentication, and reports secret NAMES and the public CDN hostname, never a value.
  - **A third secret is needed that the brief did not mention: `BUNNY_CDN_HOSTNAME`.** Bunny's playback URLs are `https://{pullzone}.b-cdn.net/{guid}/…` and the library's CDN hostname is not derivable from the Stream API key (it lives on the account-level Pull Zone API, a different credential). It is **not** a secret — it appears in every playback URL — but it is kept with the others so the edge function composes complete URLs and the client needs no constant at all, which is what lets the delivery domain change later without a client release.
  - **The Library ID cannot be kept server-side, and the brief's bullet saying otherwise is wrong on the facts.** Bunny's TUS upload requires `LibraryId` as a plain request header on the CLIENT's own request (verified against the current docs), and it appears in every playback and embed URL regardless. The API key is the secret; it never appears in a response body, a header, a log line or an error detail, and Bunny error bodies are truncated before being echoed. The brief's other bullet — "the signed upload auth headers/fields Bunny requires" — already anticipated this; the two bullets contradicted each other and this is the resolution.
  - **Playback is Bunny's MP4 fallback, not HLS, and that was a decision.** The reel viewer plays a plain `<video src>`, which cannot decode an `.m3u8` outside Safari, so HLS would have meant adding hls.js and rewriting the windowed player's lifecycle in the same PR as a new provider and a new edge function — an upload bug and a playback bug would have been indistinguishable. Bunny generates HLS for every video regardless, so moving to it later is a URL rebuild from `bunny_video_id` with no re-upload and no backfill. **MP4 Fallback must be enabled in the library's Encoding tab BEFORE the first upload** — it is not retroactive, and enabling it afterwards leaves every existing video 404ing. That trap, and the exact deploy order, are now written up in `claude.md`.
  - **`bunny_video_id` is stored alongside the URLs rather than parsed back out of them.** Deletion is `DELETE /library/{id}/videos/{guid}` — an API call, not a URL fetch — so parsing a display URL to perform a destructive call would make the URL format load-bearing for deletion, and the day it changed every vendor delete would silently leak paid storage instead of failing loudly. Reconciliation keys off the same column for the same reason. Partial index, bunny rows only. The one existing `provider='supabase'` row was deliberately not backfilled or touched.
  - **The delete path is closed, and the row is the authorization anchor.** `bunny-delete-video` takes a `product_videos.id`, resolves the row with the service-role key, and reads BOTH the owner and the GUID from that row — the `razorpay-verify-payment` pattern. Accepting a `bunny_video_id` from the request body would have been the hole: `pvideos_select` is `status='live' OR vendor_id=auth.uid() OR is_admin()`, so **every live row's GUID is readable by anon with the publishable key that ships in the bundle** — an attacker-known value, not a secret. A 404 at Bunny counts as success, which is what lets a retry after a partial failure finish instead of deadlocking.
  - **Three findings from a design stress-test were fixed before shipping**, two of them bugs this work introduced:
    - `orphan-reconciliation.sql` Query 2 had no provider filter. `split_part()` returns an EMPTY STRING (not null) when the delimiter is absent, so a Bunny URL reduced to `''`, matched no storage object, and came back `MISSING` — a query documented to mean "renders as a broken video in the buyer feed". The first run after Bunny shipped would have declared the entire Bunny catalogue broken. **Proved and fixed**: with a throwaway bunny row in a rolled-back transaction, the old shape reported it `MISSING` and the new one correctly excludes it while still reporting the real supabase row `ok`.
    - The signature window was one hour. tus-js-client resends the auth headers on EVERY PATCH, so Bunny re-validates the expiry per chunk — the expiry bounds the whole upload, not its start. 50 MB at ~15 kB/s is about 55 minutes, so a vendor on exactly the connection this feature exists to survive would upload almost the entire file and then start 401ing, with every retry failing identically. Raised to six hours, with `bunny-reconcile`'s age guard raised to eight to stay above it — and that coupling is now the *reason* the guard is sound: an upload cannot be in flight after its signature expires, which is a stronger guarantee than the storage side's "far longer than the widest plausible upload" estimate.
    - `bunny-reconcile` returned `missingAtBunny` even when the library listing was truncated at the page cap, so every row whose GUID sat on an unread page would be reported as having broken playback. It now returns `null` for that direction when truncated, with the reason in the response. `orphansAtBunny` is unaffected — the DB side is always complete.
  - **The encode window is real and the admin panel is where it bites.** A Bunny playback URL is recorded at insert time but the file only exists once encoding finishes. In the buyer app the poster falls back to the bundled placeholder via `onThumbError`; the moderation queue had no such handling, so a moderator would have seen a silent black `<video>` and a broken-image glyph — and the most likely first incident of this migration would have been **a moderator rejecting a good video for being blank**. `Cosora-Admin/src/pages/Videos.tsx` now surfaces a load failure with a "probably still encoding, wait and reopen" note and hides a failed poster instead of showing a glyph.
  - **`MAX_VIDEO_BYTES` deliberately unchanged at 50 MB.** It cannot be made provider-dependent as the code stands: the gate runs in `handleFileSelect` at file-pick, long before the provider is known. And the egress argument changes shape rather than disappearing — a Supabase overrun is a hard 402 that costs nothing, whereas a Bunny overrun is an unbounded bill and, when the balance empties, a catalogue-wide 404. Raising it is a billing decision that needs a spend alert first, not a line in this PR. `claude.md`'s "Raising MAX_VIDEO_BYTES" section is therefore now scoped to supabase-provider rows.
  - **Deployed vs repo, stated so it is not a surprise:** only `bunny-upload-url` is deployed (version 1), and only because deploying it was the sole way to answer the secrets question. It was deployed with `verify_jwt: true` and its source was read back and compared against the local file. It carries the ONE-hour window, since the six-hour change came after. All three functions must be deployed at go-live per step 3 of the checklist, which supersedes it. `bunny-delete-video` and `bunny-reconcile` are not deployed at all.
  - **Not done, and not claimable:** the fixture test. It cannot be written honestly yet — it needs a real Bunny upload, which needs the credentials; and the Playwright suite it would join is itself unrunnable because the `chatfx-*` / `rlstest-*` fixture accounts were deleted in a previous session and `seed-chat-fixtures.sql` / `seed-test-admins.sql` have not been re-run. A stress-test also flagged that a fixture vendor would additionally need a `vendor_profiles` row and at least one product, because `handleSubmit` hard-requires a tagged product. Recorded as outstanding rather than stubbed.
  - **Open, and deliberately not guessed at:** the playback URL hardcodes `play_720p.mp4`. Bunny only produces renditions that exist in the source, so a sub-720p clip from a low-end phone — this platform's actual user base — may never have that file, with the same permanent-404 symptom as forgetting the Encoding-tab setting. Settling it properly means reading `availableResolutions`/`thumbnailFileName` off the video object after encoding rather than composing URLs at insert time, which is a real design change (insert the row first, PATCH the URLs when encoding completes). It could not be settled empirically here because there is no library to test against. **This must be resolved before the first real vendor upload**, not after.
- 2026-09-05: **Phase 8 (Bunny Stream migration) — NOT started, and the reason is the gate it was given.** No files changed. Recorded here so it is not mistaken for an oversight or silently re-scoped later.
  - The phase was explicitly conditional on **confirming Bunny Stream account access and that pricing terms have not changed, at implementation time**. Neither can be confirmed from this environment: there is no Bunny account referenced anywhere in either repo, no API key in `.env` or in Supabase secrets, and no edge function to hold one. (`grep -ri bunny` finds only Big Buck Bunny sample clips in `src/data/videoCloseUps.ts` and the two places that already *name* Bunny as the future exit ramp — `technicalimplementation.md` and the `product_videos.provider` column comment from `20260730205449`.) Building the upload path against an account that may not exist would produce code nobody can run and a `provider='bunny'` value nothing can serve.
  - **Nothing in Phases 1–7 forecloses it, which was the point of doing them first.** `product_videos.provider` already exists precisely so this is a data change rather than a schema change; `fetchVideoCloseUps` and `fetchMyVideos` select `video_url`/`thumbnail_url` as opaque strings and need no query change; and none of this week's additions — `increment_video_view`, `video_likes`, `saved_videos` — reference storage at all. Only `createProductVideo`'s upload path has to branch on provider.
  - **What still needs deciding when it is picked up**, so it is not rediscovered: whether to store Bunny's playback URL directly or store `bunny_video_id` and resolve URLs at read time (the second survives a change of Bunny delivery domain without a backfill; the first is a smaller diff); and that the canvas-probe poster generation must be kept for `provider='supabase'` rows even once Bunny auto-generates posters for its own. The existing `'supabase'`-provider row is **not** to be backfilled — it is the working reference case, and backfilling one test row while everything else in the phase is new and unverified trades a known-good baseline for nothing.
  - **The one genuinely new piece of privileged infrastructure it needs** is a Supabase Edge Function holding the Bunny API key server-side to mint direct-upload URLs. That is justified — a key that must never reach the client — but it is also the first non-payment secret this app would hold, so it deserves its own review rather than arriving inside a feature PR.
- 2026-09-05: **Phase 0 (Cosora-Admin Video Closeups queue) confirmed shipped, not re-done.** No files changed in this repo. The working tree of `Cosora-Admin` already carried the whole phase — `src/pages/Videos.tsx`, the `"videos"` section in `roles.ts`, the `/videos` route and nav entry — and `product_videos` and its columns were already typed in that repo's `database.types.ts`. Verified from this side rather than assumed: `select status, count(*) from product_videos group by status` returns exactly one row, `rejected: 1`. That is the month-old `under_review` video the phase existed to unblock — approved, watched, found not to match its own listing, and rejected with a reason. So the buyer feed correctly shows no real videos today, which is the right outcome and not an empty-state bug.
  - **One correction to the record.** `Cosora-Admin/README.md` still said `20260905170000_approve_vendor_content_clears_rejection_reason.sql` was written but **not applied** (MCP was down that session). It is applied — the ledger has it as version `20260905172020`, and both `approve_vendor_content` and `approve_vendor_content_bulk` now carry `rejection_reason = null` in their SET clauses, confirmed by reading `pg_get_functiondef` off the live database. Corrected in that repo's new `CHANGELOG.md`.
- 2026-09-05: **Phase 7 — the 90 MB / Pro-plan upgrade path written down before it is needed.** `documentation/claude.md`. No code change. New "Raising MAX_VIDEO_BYTES" section giving the order explicitly: upgrade the org to Pro, **then raise Storage → Settings → Global file size limit in the dashboard, then** bump `MAX_VIDEO_BYTES` in `src/lib/queries/videos.ts` and the `product-videos` bucket's `file_size_limit` together. The dashboard step is first because it is the only one that cannot be scripted — there is no SQL, no Storage API call and no MCP tool for a project-level setting — and because **a bucket's `file_size_limit` can never exceed the project's global limit**. Doing it in the wrong order is worse than doing nothing: the bucket accepts the higher number, uploads keep failing at 50 MB citing the bucket, and the client constant advertises a ceiling the platform will not honour — so a vendor picks an 80 MB file, sits through a long resumable upload on a phone connection, and is rejected at the end. Recorded as a rule in the business-rules list too, since the failure is silent.
- 2026-09-05: **Phase 6 — a renamed `.mov` no longer passes the upload gate.** `src/lib/queries/videos.ts` (new `sniffVideoContainer`), `src/pages/UploadVideo.tsx`. `file.type` is the OS's guess from the extension, so renaming an iPhone `.mov` to `.mp4` made it report `video/mp4`, satisfied `ACCEPTED_VIDEO_MIME`, satisfied the bucket's server-side allowlist (which checks the same declared type), and landed in the buyer feed as a clip that plays on Safari and paints a black rectangle on Chrome and Android — most buyers here. `handleFileSelect` now reads the first 12 bytes after the MIME and size checks and before the duration probe.
  - **The discriminator is the major brand, not the presence of an `ftyp` box.** QuickTime is ISO-BMFF too and has one; checking for `ftyp` alone would have passed every `.mov`. Bytes 8–12 carry the brand, and `'qt  '` is QuickTime. WebM is caught separately on the EBML magic (`1A 45 DF A3`). The rejection toast names the actual problem ("This is a QuickTime (.mov) file renamed to .mp4") and reuses the existing "re-export as MP4 (H.264)" remedy from the duration-probe failure.
  - **Explicitly a stopgap, and the comment says so.** HEVC muxed into a genuine MP4 container (brand `isom`) still passes, and a hand-built header on any payload always will. Those need a decoder to reject, which is Phase 8's transcoder. This narrows the accidental case — a phone renaming a file — which is the realistic failure mode, not deliberate evasion. Recorded that way in `claude.md` rather than as "closed".
- 2026-09-05: **Phase 5 — storage/row reconciliation for `product-videos`, as a reviewed query rather than a scheduled deleter.** New `documentation/orphan-reconciliation.sql`. `deleteProductVideo()` already removes objects before the row (the row being the only record of where the files live), which covers the app's own delete path but not an admin's direct-SQL delete, a failed insert after a successful upload, or the tus-fingerprint case that produced a real 30 MB orphan once already.
  - **Two queries, both read-only.** Query 1 lists bucket objects no row references; Query 2 lists rows whose `video_url`/`thumbnail_url` point at objects that are gone — the same failure modes tend to produce both, and a broken poster is as visible to a buyer as a missing video. Matching is on the storage **key** extracted with `split_part`, not on the whole URL and not with `LIKE`: the project hostname is environment-dependent, and `_` inside a filename is a `LIKE` wildcard.
  - **The `created_at < now() - interval '1 hour'` guard is load-bearing.** `createProductVideo` uploads the video, then the poster, then inserts the row — a multi-second window where the object legitimately has no row, and far longer for a resumed upload. Any reconciliation without that guard eventually deletes a video mid-upload.
  - **Not automated, and the file says why.** An orphan costs a few MB on a plan measured in GB; a wrongly deleted object is a vendor's video gone with no undo. The costs are not symmetric, so the default is list-and-look. The file also warns against `delete from storage.objects` — the row is not the file, and deleting it directly strands the object while removing the only handle on it.
  - **Verified against the live project:** 0 orphans, the one real row's video and poster both resolve `ok`, and the key extraction reproduces exactly the two object names actually in the bucket.
- 2026-09-05: **Phase 4 — the reel finally reads the buyer's stored preferences, not just this session's bookmarks.** `src/lib/buyerCategories.ts` (new `preferredVideoCategoryNames`), `src/pages/VideoCloseUpsPage.tsx`, `src/pages/NewArrivals.tsx`. Both pages built `interestedCategories` from `bookmarkedVideoIds` alone, so a signed-in buyer whose account has carried `buyer_profiles.preferred_categories` since registration got a generic reel until they bookmarked something. That column is real, populated, and already drives `products.ts`'s `useYouMightLike` — the reel was the one surface ignoring it. Now seeded from it, with session bookmarks folded on top.
  - **The taxonomy question was checked, not assumed.** `product_videos.category` is plain `text` defaulting to `'Apparel'`, **not** an FK like `products.category_id`, so there was no guarantee it shared the `PREF_TO_DB_CATEGORY_NAMES` vocabulary. It does: `createProductVideo` populates it by copying the tagged product's `categories.name`, and `UploadVideo` refuses to submit without a tagged product — the one live row's category is `"Buttons"`, a genuine `categories.name`. So the video side wants the same map's **names**, one step earlier than `resolvePreferredCategoryIds`, which goes on to trade those names for ids. Hence a separate tiny helper rather than reusing that function, and no DB round-trip at all.
  - **The two defaults (`'Apparel'` server-side, `'Fashion'` in `createProductVideo`'s fallback) match no preference**, which is correct rather than a gap — an untagged video carries no category signal — but it is written down as the reason this is a lookup and not an assumption that every row maps to something.
- 2026-09-05: **Phase 3 — a saved video survives a refresh.** New migration `20260905175109_saved_videos.sql` (**applied**), new `src/lib/queries/videoEngagement.ts`, `src/components/buyer/VideoCloseUpsViewer.tsx`, `src/lib/database.types.ts`. The bookmark in the viewer was a `useState<Set<string>>` and nothing else: a refresh lost it, a second device never had it, and it did not match how a saved *product* already behaves. `saved_videos` mirrors `saved_items` exactly — composite PK, `saved_videos_owner` as `for all using (buyer_id = auth.uid()) with check (buyer_id = auth.uid())`, fire-and-forget upsert/delete from the client.
  - **Not bolted onto `savedStore.ts`, and that is the interesting decision.** That store's entire state shape is folders-of-`ListingProduct`s (`saved_items` + `saved_folders` + `saved_folder_items`, with a permanent "All Saves"). A saved video has no folder — the viewer has one bookmark button and no folder picker — so adding it there meant either inventing a folder model the UI does not have or special-casing every selector. The DB shape is deliberately identical; only the client state model differs, which is what `lib/queries/videoEngagement.ts` exists to say.
  - **The in-session bookmark set stays, and stays separate.** `onBookmarkChange` still reports only taps made in this viewer session, and hydrated saves are deliberately **not** folded into it: `rankVideoCloseUps` wants "what caught their eye in the last few minutes", and seeding it from durable saves would let a save from six weeks ago reshuffle today's reel. Two signals, two questions — now recorded as a business rule.
  - Synthetic dev-sample ids (`dev1`) are guarded by `isPersistableVideoId`, the same way `savedStore` guards synthetic product ids against a uuid FK.
- 2026-09-05: **Phase 2 — real view and like counters, and `views_count` stops being a dead sort key.** New migration `20260905175044_video_view_and_like_counters.sql` (**applied**), `src/lib/queries/videoEngagement.ts`, `src/components/buyer/VideoCloseUpsViewer.tsx`. `fetchVideoCloseUps` has always ordered by `views_count`; every live row sat at 0, so the ordering was an arbitrary tiebreak. Nothing incremented it because a buyer owns no video row and RLS gives them no UPDATE — the same reason `increment_product_view` exists for products.
  - **`increment_video_view(p uuid)` is `increment_product_view` verbatim**, down to `language sql` and the parameter name, scoped to `status = 'live'` server-side so passing a draft/rejected id is a silent no-op. **One deliberate difference:** it revokes PUBLIC and grants only `authenticated, anon`. `increment_product_view` still carries a stray PUBLIC grant from before that convention; not touched here, since that is a products change.
  - **A view is monotonic; a like is a toggle — so likes are NOT a twin RPC.** A counter-only `increment_video_like` would give no way to undo a like, no way to answer "did I already like this?" tomorrow, and no source of truth to rebuild from — the heart would be a per-session lie on top of a number that only grows. Likes therefore get `video_likes`, a per-buyer join table with the same RLS shape as `saved_items`, and `product_videos.likes_count` is maintained by an AFTER INSERT/DELETE trigger (`sync_video_likes_count`, SECURITY DEFINER because the liking buyer has no UPDATE on `product_videos`). `greatest(likes_count - 1, 0)` floors it: `likes_count` predates this table and is not derived from it, so an unlike must not push a legitimately-zero row negative.
  - **The moderation trigger does not interfere and it is worth knowing why.** `enforce_product_videos_moderation` short-circuits on `current_user <> 'authenticated'`, and a SECURITY DEFINER function owned by `postgres` runs as `postgres`. It would not have fired regardless — it guards only `status` and `rejection_reason` — but the counter path never even reaches `is_admin()`.
  - **The displayed like count is now `base + delta`, not `base + (liked ? 1 : 0)`.** Once likes are durable, `video.likes` already contains a like made yesterday, so the old expression would count it twice the moment the feed refetched — and could not express an unlike at all. The viewer hydrates what the DB said on open and displays the stored count plus this session's *change* to it.
  - **Views are counted on the ACTIVE slide only (distance 0).** A slide at distance 1 has a `<video>` mounted and metadata preloading but nobody watching it; counting those would make `views_count` a measure of scroll velocity rather than attention. Dedup is two-layer — an in-memory `Set` plus `sessionStorage` — because unlike a product page, a reel slide re-activates every time you scroll back to it and remounts as it re-enters `SLIDE_WINDOW`. `recordProductView`'s bare `sessionStorage` check would have over-counted here.
  - **`sync_video_likes_count` had PUBLIC EXECUTE and no longer does** (`revoke_public_execute_on_sync_video_likes_count`, ledger `20260905182854`, folded into the same file so it stays self-contained). Calling a trigger function directly raises 'can only be called as a trigger', so this was not exploitable — but SECURITY DEFINER plus PUBLIC EXECUTE is the shape a reviewer stops on, and the moderation RPCs already follow the tighter convention. Postgres checks EXECUTE at `CREATE TRIGGER` time rather than on each fire, so the revoke cannot break the trigger; re-verified live afterwards anyway — a like still sets `likes_count` to 1, an unlike still returns it to 0.
  - **Both new migration files are named for the version the ledger actually recorded** (`20260905175044`, `20260905175109`), not for a locally-invented timestamp. That is a departure from the rest of this folder, where MCP-applied migrations were given fresh remote versions that no local filename matches — the reconciliation problem the 2026-09-05 chat-pipeline entry describes. It does not fix that backlog, it just stops these two adding to it.
  - **Verified live in one rolled-back transaction, 5 assertions:** a view on a live row increments (2 calls → 2), a view on the `rejected` row does not (stays 0), a like row sets `likes_count` to 1, deleting it returns it to 0, an unlike from 0 floors at 0 rather than going negative, and deleting the video cascades both `video_likes` and `saved_videos` away without the AFTER-delete trigger erroring. Database confirmed unchanged afterwards — the real row still reads `views_count = 0`.
- 2026-09-05: **Phase 1 — the two undone items from Part 9: a rejection reason a vendor can actually read, and a placeholder that is not someone else's server.** `src/lib/queries/videos.ts`, `src/pages/UploadVideo.tsx`, new `src/assets/placeholders/video-thumb-placeholder.svg`.
  - **The rejection reason existed everywhere except where the vendor could see it.** `reject_vendor_content` stored it and the BEFORE trigger protected it, but `useMyVideos`'s select never fetched the column and `MyVideoRow` never typed it — so a rejected badge was a dead end: resubmit, get rejected again, learn nothing. Now selected, typed, threaded through `fetchMyVideos`'s mapping, and rendered under the status badge. There is **no vendor-facing equivalent for a rejected product** anywhere in this repo to mirror (`rejection_reason` appears only in `database.types.ts`), so the simple "Rejected: &lt;reason&gt;" line was the right shape.
  - **Rendering is gated on `status === "rejected"`, not on the column alone** — a rejected row keeps its note through a resubmit until an approve clears it (`approve_vendor_content` nulls it as of `20260905172020`), so reading the column by itself would caption an under-review card with a stale rejection. Same guard Cosora-Admin's `Products.tsx`/`Videos.tsx` adopted for the same reason.
  - **Both hotlinked Unsplash placeholders are gone**, replaced by a 652-byte bundled SVG (`THUMB_PLACEHOLDER` in `videos.ts`, and `THUMB_PLACEHOLDER` plus the phone-mockup fallback in `UploadVideo.tsx`). This one sits on the critical path of every reel card without a poster, and it is what the `<video>` paints underneath before the first frame decodes — a blocked or slow third-party fetch there shows as a black rectangle. Confirmed in the production bundle: it inlines as a `data:image/svg+xml` URI, so it costs zero requests. (`products.ts`'s own `PLACEHOLDER` is still an Unsplash URL — out of scope here, flagged as still open.)
- 2026-09-05: **Chat-pipeline pass closed out — T10.3 fixed live, fixtures purged, and the local migration folder reconciled against what is actually applied.** `documentation/test.md`, `documentation/changelog.md`; **deleted** `supabase/migrations/20260905120000_notifications_read_only_except_read_flag.sql`. Both database changes were made **outside this repo**, through Supabase's MCP tools in a separate Claude session — the same route by which earlier "written but not yet applied" entries in this changelog were eventually closed, since neither repo holds a service-role key by design.
  - **T10.3 is closed.** Migration `restrict_notifications_update_to_read_column` is live: a BEFORE UPDATE trigger `enforce_notification_update_columns` on `public.notifications` rejecting any UPDATE that changes `title` / `body` / `kind` / `conversation_id` / `profile_id`, leaving `read` as the only column an owner may write. Verified live as an authenticated user — updating `read` succeeds; updating `title` raises `42501: Only the read column may be changed on a notification`. That closes the gap where `notifications_mark_read`'s RLS (`profile_id = auth.uid()`) could see **which row** but not **which column** — the reason the fix had to be a trigger, exactly as for `products.status`, `profiles.account_status` and `conversations.status`.
  - **The local migration file was deleted rather than kept.** `20260905120000_notifications_read_only_except_read_flag.sql` did the same job under different object names (`enforce_notification_updates()` / `trg_notifications_read_only`). Left in place, a future `supabase db push` would install a **second, redundant trigger** alongside the live one rather than recognising it as already applied — its own `drop trigger if exists` names the local trigger, so it would not have replaced the remote one. The reasoning it carried is preserved in commit `8bca619`.
  - **One deliberate narrowing, recorded rather than silently dropped.** The deleted local version also guarded `id` and `created_at`; the applied version does not. Neither is client-settable in any current write path, and the security-relevant columns — the ones that would let a client forge *"Your account has been reinstated"* — are covered identically. Flagged here so the difference is a decision on the record, not a discrepancy someone rediscovers.
  - **This repo has never been linked to the Supabase project, and local filenames do not match the remote migration history.** `npx supabase migration list` fails with `LegacyProjectNotLinkedError`, so `migration list` / `db pull` could not be used to diff. Beyond the one file above, every migration applied through MCP this week was recorded remotely under the **name** passed to `apply_migration` with a fresh version timestamp, not under the local filename — so `20260802130000_notifications.sql` and its siblings are live but will not be recognised by version if anyone links this repo and runs `db push`. **Nobody should run `supabase db push` against this project until that history is reconciled** (link, `db pull`, then repair `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations`). Not attempted here: it needs an access token and DB password this environment does not have.
  - **All 10 known-password fixture accounts are gone.** `chatfx-buyer-a/b`, `chatfx-vendor-a/b` and the six `rlstest-*` admins, plus everything they touched: 84 `conversation_reviews`, 18 `account_suspensions`, 178 `notifications`, 8 `messages`, 4 `conversations`, 2 `vendor_profiles`, 5 `admin_flags`. Verified after: **0 leftover fixture users**, `real_admins` back to **2**, and the 4 conversations / 8 messages still present belong to real non-fixture accounts and were untouched. Deleted directly; `drop-chat-fixtures.sql` and `drop-test-admins.sql` were **not** run and would now no-op. That closes the outstanding risk flagged in the previous entry — admin logins with a known password sitting in a live database.
  - **The chat-pipeline suite is therefore not runnable as-is.** Every case in `chat-pipeline-matrix.mjs` and both Playwright specs depends on those accounts, so `Cosora-Admin/scripts/seed-chat-fixtures.sql` **and** `seed-test-admins.sql` must be re-run before the suite executes again. The seed scripts are unchanged and still correct; only the rows are gone.
  - **T10.3 is marked PASS on the live verification quoted above, not on a matrix re-run.** The fixtures that case needs were deleted in the same cleanup, so `chat-pipeline-matrix.mjs` could not re-execute it. `documentation/test.md` says so explicitly in the T10.3 row rather than implying the harness re-confirmed it — the distinction matters, because "the fix looks correct" is precisely how the `vendor_profiles.account_status` regression survived undetected.
- 2026-09-05: **Chat pipeline tested at both layers for the first time — and the UI layer was hiding a blank page.** New `playwright.config.ts`, `tests/chat-pipeline.spec.ts`, `tests/admin-chat-moderation.spec.ts`, `screenshots/chat-pipeline/`; new `supabase/migrations/20260905120000_notifications_read_only_except_read_flag.sql`; `src/lib/queries/notifications.ts`, `documentation/test.md`. In Cosora-Admin: new `scripts/chat-pipeline-matrix.mjs`, `scripts/seed-chat-fixtures.sql`, `scripts/drop-chat-fixtures.sql`, and a fix to `scripts/seed-test-admins.sql`. 72 DB cases + 9 Playwright cases, T1–T13.
  - **`/notifications` was a white screen, and every database check said it was fine.** Opening the page threw *"cannot add `postgres_changes` callbacks for realtime:notifications:&lt;uid&gt; after `subscribe()`"*; the error escaped the effect, killed the React tree, and rendered an empty body. `supabase.channel(name)` **returns an existing channel** when one with that topic is open, and two components use `useNotifications()` at once on that route — `DashboardHeader` via `useUnreadCount()`, and the page itself — so the second mount called `.on()` on the first mount's already-subscribed channel. React 18 StrictMode's double-invoked effects reach it alone. Fixed with a per-instance channel topic; React Query already dedupes the data, so the only cost is a second socket.
  - **This is the exact failure this pass existed to find.** `notifications-check.mjs` passed 6/6 earlier the same day, the rows were correct in the table throughout, and the RLS was right. Nobody had opened the page in a browser. A feature is not verified until both layers are.
  - **A client can rewrite its own notification's `title`, `body` and `kind`.** `notifications_mark_read` constrains WHICH ROW (`profile_id = auth.uid()`) and says nothing about WHICH COLUMN. Confirmed with a real login: the row came back reading `title = "TAMPERED"`, `kind = "account_reinstated"`. That matters because the table has **no INSERT policy for any role** and `notify()` has no EXECUTE grant, precisely so a client cannot manufacture *"Your account has been reinstated"* — unconstrained UPDATE hands that back through the side door, same row. Fix written as a BEFORE UPDATE trigger (RLS sees rows, not columns — the same division of labour `products.status`, `profiles.account_status` and `conversations.status` already use). **The migration is NOT applied**: the Supabase MCP connection dropped mid-pass and neither repo holds a service-role key, by design. Left as a FAIL in `documentation/test.md` rather than marked PASS on the strength of the fix looking right.
  - **The test harness poisoned its own probes, twice, before it found anything real.** `TAG` was `chatfx-${Date.now()}` — a 13-digit timestamp contains a 10-digit run starting 6-9, which is exactly what the seeded *Indian mobile number* pattern matches (`select 'chatfx-1788604604310' ~* '(\+?91[\-\s]?)?[6-9]\d{9}\y'` → **true**). Every T3 probe was flagged by the phone pattern first, and first-match-wins filed the review against the wrong pattern id. A fixture must not be matchable by the rules it is testing. Separately, T2 did not unlock the thread first, so a lock left by an earlier run made an RLS refusal indistinguishable from a blocklist hit — both are `42501` — and three cases failed for a reason that had nothing to do with the blocklist.
  - **Two assertions were passing without testing anything.** T7.6b set `conversations.status` to the value the row already held, so `enforce_conversation_status`'s `is distinct from` guard never fired, the UPDATE was a legal no-op, and 1 row came back looking like a successful bypass. And T2.6/T3 counted `conversation_reviews` absolutely, which measures history rather than the test — that table has no DELETE policy for any client, so earlier runs' rows cannot be cleared. Both now assert deltas across a single action.
  - **`seed-test-admins.sql` could not run at all.** It inserted `vendor_profiles.account_status`, a column `20260801095820` dropped — so the repo's own fixture seeder had been broken since that migration landed.
  - **The suspension scope regression is now guarded.** T8 suspends a vendor holding a **live** product, an **active** ad and an existing review, and asserts none of them is hidden, paused or altered — `product live→live, ad active→active, review intact`. That is the distinction that makes the current INSERT-only scope safe, and the exact thing a later "helpful" widening to UPDATE would silently break by taking down a paying vendor's campaign. The fixture carries a **live gold subscription** for the same reason: without it `enforce_plan_limits` raises `P0001` from a BEFORE trigger before RLS is consulted, and the advertisement case reports DENY whether or not the gate works.
  - **The `p_resume` trap is real, and the UI does not fall into it.** `resolve_conversation_review(verdict='resumed')` with `p_resume` omitted leaves the thread `under_review` (T6.3b) — the function's default is FALSE. The admin Resume button was then driven for real and `conversations.status` read back out of the database afterwards: it returns to `active` (T6.3). A UI that forgot the flag would have shown the same success toast over a still-locked thread.
  - **Everything else held.** Blocklist is a hard stop with no review trail and no lock; regex flags keep the message and file exactly one review; `reported_reason` is stored verbatim with `reason_id` left null; double-resolve raises `P0002`; `account_suspensions` is genuinely append-only for every role including super_admin; nobody — owner, admin, or plain user — can UPDATE `conversations.status` or `profiles.account_status` directly, or UPDATE/DELETE `messages`; suspension and conversation lock are still independent axes; contact details are gated in all four states with registry data still visible; and the four non-chat admin roles are refused both in the nav and by direct API call.
  - **Behaviour newly documented rather than assumed:** reporting an already-locked conversation **succeeds and files a second review row** (T4.3). Defensible — two complaints are two facts — but one thread can accumulate many pending reviews and the queue shows each.
  - **Re-confirmed, not assumed:** `useCallBuyer()` still deliberately does not log to `calls` (its insert policy is `buyer_id = auth.uid()`, unsatisfiable by a vendor caller); there is still no buyer-side contact card to mirror the vendor one; seed service vendors and freelancers still have no `profiles` row and so are still not suspension-gated (R-23). No other asymmetry appeared — every buyer→vendor case has a vendor→buyer twin with an identical result.
  - **Test hygiene.** Dedicated fixtures only (`chatfx-*@cosora.test`, ids `cf00000*`), never the demo accounts — `messages` has no DELETE policy for any role, so every probe message is permanent, and a crashed run would leave a demo account suspended. Blocklist terms were throwaway and deleted; the production blocklist is still empty as designed. Final state verified: 0 blocklist terms, 3 flag patterns, 7 block reasons, 0 suspended profiles anywhere, 0 locked conversations anywhere, 0 pending reviews.
  - **Outstanding, needs a service-role session:** apply `20260905120000`, re-run `node Cosora-Admin/scripts/chat-pipeline-matrix.mjs`, flip T10.3 only if it passes — then run `Cosora-Admin/scripts/drop-chat-fixtures.sql` and `drop-test-admins.sql`. The fixture and `rlstest-*` accounts are still in the live database, and they are logins with a known password.
  - `tests/new-arrivals.spec.ts` still fails on its own pre-existing reason (no `[role="tab"]` on `/home/new-arrivals`), unchanged by the new config and already recorded as failing.
# Changelog

Reverse-chronological log of every change, maintained automatically after every
prompt/session that changes anything — no manual request needed.

Entry format:

```
## YYYY-MM-DD — <short title>
- What changed:
- Why:
- Files touched:
```

> **Note on older entries.** Everything below the 2026-09-05 entry predates this format and
> is kept verbatim as `- YYYY-MM-DD: <title>` with nested bullets. It is still
> reverse-chronological. Do not reformat it; write new entries in the format above.

---

## 2026-09-05 — Approve no longer leaves a stale rejection reason on a live row

- **What changed:** `approve_vendor_content(target_table, target_id)` and
  `approve_vendor_content_bulk(target)` set `status = 'live'` but never touched
  `rejection_reason` — a video that was rejected, edited back to `under_review`
  by its vendor, then re-approved went live still carrying the old moderator
  note. New migration
  `supabase/migrations/20260905170000_approve_vendor_content_clears_rejection_reason.sql`
  (`CREATE OR REPLACE`, both functions) adds `rejection_reason = null` to the
  `products` and `product_videos` branches of each. **`catalogues` is
  deliberately left as `status = 'live'` only** — it has no `rejection_reason`
  column (confirmed live: `SELECT rejection_reason FROM catalogues` →
  `42703 undefined_column`), and `reject_vendor_content` already carries the
  identical exclusion for the same reason. Adding the clause there would not
  fix a display bug, it would `42703` on every approval for a vendor with a
  pending catalogue — and because a PL/pgSQL function body has no implicit
  per-statement savepoint, that error would abort the whole call and roll back
  the `products`/`product_videos` updates that ran earlier in the same
  transaction, turning a cosmetic bug into "moderators can no longer approve
  anything for a vendor who also has a pending catalogue."
- **In `Cosora-Admin`**, the display side of the same bug: `Products.tsx` and
  `Videos.tsx` both rendered `{row.rejection_reason && (<red banner>)}` with no
  `status` check, so a live row carrying a stale reason showed a "Rejection
  reason:" banner under its green "live" badge. Both now read
  `{row.status === "rejected" && row.rejection_reason && (...)}`.
  `VendorDetail.tsx` was checked too — it renders vendor profile fields only,
  never `rejection_reason`, so nothing there needed changing.
- **Also in `Cosora-Admin`:** the dev server 404'd on `/favicon.ico` — no
  `public/` directory exists and `index.html` declared no icon at all. Added an
  inlined `data:image/svg+xml;base64,...` favicon (no new file, no `public/`
  dir needed) that echoes the real monogram from `src/components/ui.tsx`'s
  `<Logo>` (near-black rounded tile, gradient `#1d1d20`→`#050506`, bold white
  "C", the selvedge stripe), rather than a generic placeholder icon.
- **Reproduced end-to-end and confirmed fixed against the live app**, using
  throwaway `product_videos` rows created, exercised and deleted in the same
  session (zero storage objects either row, since neither carried a real
  upload): insert → `reject_vendor_content` → vendor PATCH back to
  `under_review` (confirmed: `rejection_reason` survives that transition,
  nothing clears it) → `approve_vendor_content_bulk` → resulting row was
  `status: 'live'` with the stale reason still attached, and loading
  `Cosora-Admin`'s Live tab against that exact row showed no rejection banner
  once the display fix was in place. **Not yet confirmed**: the migration
  itself. The Supabase MCP connection was down for this entire session (every
  MCP tool call failed, matching the same disconnect noted in the 2026-09-05
  chat-pipeline entries above), and neither repo holds a service-role key or a
  linked Supabase CLI project — so the `CREATE OR REPLACE` in the migration
  file has not been applied to the live database. The exact throwaway-row
  sequence above is what proves it once it is: `rejection_reason` should come
  back `null` on the resulting live row instead of carrying the stale reason.
- **Also completed:** the specific mismatched video flagged for takedown
  (`product_videos.id = 35783726-439f-4e02-b2fe-3cd2eda8f707`, "Yoyoyo") was
  still `live` at the start of this session; rejected via
  `reject_vendor_content('product_videos', ..., 'Test content — footage does
  not match tagged product/category')` and confirmed `status = 'rejected'`
  with that exact reason stored.
- **Files touched:** `supabase/migrations/20260905170000_...sql` (new, this
  repo, not yet applied — see above). In `Cosora-Admin`:
  `src/pages/Products.tsx`, `src/pages/Videos.tsx`, `index.html`.

## 2026-09-05 — Cosora-Admin can moderate Video Closeups at all (Phase 0)

- **What changed:** Added a Video Closeups moderation queue to `Cosora-Admin`. New
  `src/pages/Videos.tsx` (route `/videos`, nav entry, three tabs: queue / live / rejected),
  a `"videos"` section in `src/lib/roles.ts` with `SECTION_READ` =
  super_admin · product_moderator · support and `SECTION_WRITE` = super_admin ·
  product_moderator, and the matching `RequireSection` route in `src/App.tsx` and rail item
  in `src/components/Shell.tsx`. Nothing in this repo changed — no schema, no RLS, no
  trigger, no migration. The gates this screen leans on all shipped on 2026-08-01.
- **Why:** The moderation *machinery* for `product_videos` has existed since
  `20260801085830` — default `under_review`, a BEFORE trigger, per-item approve and reject
  RPCs, a `rejection_reason` column — but no operator surface ever consumed it. One real
  vendor video (`brand_line "Yoyoyo"`, uploaded 2026-08-04) had sat `under_review` for a
  month with no screen anywhere able to approve or reject it. A moderation gate nobody can
  open is a queue that fills up, not a control.
- **Design decisions worth keeping:**
  - **Raw `.update()` + `assertWrote`, not `approve_vendor_content` / `reject_vendor_content`.**
    Both RPCs exist and are correctly role-gated, but Products, Ads and VendorDetail all
    write the column directly and let RLS + the trigger refuse it. One convention beats two.
    The cost is the RPC's `P0002` "you may only approve something that is `under_review`"
    guard, which the UI supplies exactly as `Products.tsx` does — Approve is not rendered
    for a live row, and each tab holds one status.
  - **The bulk button IS an RPC**, because vendor-wide approval has no column to write.
    `approve_vendor_content_bulk` returns `void`, so there is nothing to `assertWrote` and
    no count to report; the page counts that vendor's pending videos either side of the
    call and reports what actually moved. Its confirm dialog names the real blast radius —
    products **and** videos **and** catalogues, including items nobody looked at — because
    the RPC's reach is wider than the screen it is invoked from.
  - **A moderator can watch the clip in-panel.** Moderating video off a poster frame is
    theatre. The `<video>` mounts only when the modal opens, so the list costs one poster
    JPEG per row rather than an mp4 — the 5 GB/month egress ceiling is shared with the
    buyer app.
  - **A row with no `video_url` is called out in red**, not left silently approvable. That
    is the signature the resumed-upload bug left behind (row recorded one path, bytes went
    to another), and approving one publishes a broken player to buyers.
  - **No `<FlagLog />`, deliberately.** `admin_flags_entity_type_check` allows
    `('vendor','product','ad','conversation')` — `'video'` would `23514` on the first note
    saved. Adding it is a one-line constraint migration in Cosora-Admin, out of scope here.
- **Verified in a real browser against the live project, not by reading the code:**
  signed in as `super_admin`; the queue rendered the real pending video with its vendor,
  wait time, tagged product, length and pixel size; the clip decoded and played
  (`readyState 4`, 478×850, `currentTime` advancing); **the real video was approved** and
  then confirmed playing in the **signed-out** buyer feed at `/video-closeups`
  (`readyState 4`, HTTP 206 range streaming, `currentTime` 14.49 → 17.07 over 2.5 s), with
  no `[DEV]` sample clips present. Reject, the required-reason gate, the stored reason
  rendering back in the audit tab, the missing-poster fallback, the no-`video_url` notice
  and the bulk approve were all exercised on a throwaway row seeded as `demo-vendor` and
  **deleted afterwards** (`product_videos` back to exactly one row, zero storage objects).
  The four database gates were re-proven with a real vendor JWT along the way: insert with
  `status:'live'` is downgraded to `under_review`, a pre-seeded `rejection_reason` is
  nulled, and both a vendor `PATCH` to `live` and a vendor `PATCH` of `rejection_reason`
  are refused `42501`.
- **Files touched:** all in `Cosora-Admin` — `src/pages/Videos.tsx` (new),
  `src/lib/roles.ts`, `src/App.tsx`, `src/components/Shell.tsx`, `README.md`. In this repo:
  `documentation/changelog.md`, `documentation/sides.md`, `documentation/sitemap.md`.
  `Cosora-Admin` typecheck and `vite build` both clean.


## 2026-09-05 — Documentation system moved into `documentation/`

- **What changed:** Introduced a persistent documentation system. Created
  `documentation/` at the repo root; `git mv`'d `CLAUDE.md` → `documentation/claude.md`
  and `changelog.md` → `documentation/changelog.md`; rewrote `documentation/claude.md`
  around a Project-Memory template (positioning, predefined vs. discovered business rules,
  architecture snapshot, pointers, Documentation Protocol) with all previous technical
  depth relocated rather than discarded. Added `documentation/technicalimplementation.md`
  (stack, architecture, data model, key modules, integrations, and the full verbatim
  invariants/tech-debt list from the old CLAUDE.md), `documentation/sitemap.md` (all 97
  routes grouped by side, extracted from `src/App.tsx`), `documentation/sides.md`
  (buyer/vendor/admin scope, migrated from `cosora.md`) and `documentation/test.md`
  (Playwright suite + the verification scripts under `scripts/`). Root `CLAUDE.md` is now a
  one-line `@documentation/claude.md` import. Updated the `UserPromptSubmit` and `Stop`
  hooks plus the `PreCompact` agent prompt in `.claude/settings.json` to the new paths.
- **Why:** Project context was concentrated in one 38 KB CLAUDE.md plus a 345 KB changelog,
  with no separation between "read this first" baseline and reference depth, and the
  `memory/` directory those files told every session to read does not exist in this repo.
  Splitting by concern keeps session-start context small while making the protocol for
  keeping it current explicit and unprompted.
- **Files touched:** `CLAUDE.md` (now a stub), `documentation/claude.md` (moved +
  rewritten), `documentation/changelog.md` (moved + header), `documentation/test.md`,
  `documentation/technicalimplementation.md`, `documentation/sitemap.md`,
  `documentation/sides.md`, `.claude/settings.json`.
- **Hook bugs found and fixed while repointing `.claude/settings.json`:**
  1. The `UserPromptSubmit` hook invoked **`pwsh`, which is not installed on this
     machine** — it had been failing silently, so no project context was ever injected.
     Now `powershell`.
  2. It built its payload with a **single-quoted PowerShell here-string** (`@'` … `'@`),
     which is literal — so even had `pwsh` existed it would have injected the characters
     `$claudemd` rather than the file's contents. Replaced with string concatenation.
  3. Added `-Encoding UTF8` to `Get-Content`; without it PS 5.1 reads the UTF-8 doc as
     ANSI and mangles every em dash.
  4. The hook **no longer injects the changelog**. At 348 KB, injecting it on every
     prompt contradicts protocol step 1 (read only `claude.md` for baseline); the other
     documentation files are named in the injected text so they can be opened on demand.
  Verified end to end: the hook now emits valid JSON carrying 12,438 characters of
  context with correct punctuation, and the `Stop` hook's JSON parses.
- **Note:** `cosora.md` at the repo root is now fully superseded by
  `documentation/sides.md` and `documentation/claude.md`; it was left in place rather than
