  deleted. Its "Admin side: not designed, not built" and "Backend: not started" lines are
  stale — both are false as of this date.

- 2026-09-05: **Chat moderation 3 — the lock got an unlock, the pipeline got seeded, moderation started telling people, and suspension started meaning something.** New `supabase/migrations/{20260802130000_notifications,20260802130100_notify_from_moderation_functions,20260802130200_seed_chat_moderation_rules,20260802150000_suspension_blocks_content_creation}.sql`; new `src/lib/queries/notifications.ts`, `scripts/{notifications-check,suspension-gate-check,contact-gate-check}.mjs`; `src/lib/notificationsStore.ts`, `src/pages/Notifications.tsx`, `src/components/layout/DashboardHeader.tsx`, `src/lib/database.types.ts` regenerated, `CLAUDE.md`. The `resolve_conversation_review` and `regex_probe` RPCs live in Cosora-Admin's migrations.
  - **A locked conversation could not be unlocked. At all.** `enforce_conversation_status()` permits support/super_admin to change `conversations.status`, so it read as done — but `conversations_update`'s USING clause is participants-only, and **a trigger cannot grant visibility**. An admin's UPDATE matched zero rows and PostgREST returned success, so the panel would have reported a resumed chat that was still locked. Fixed with a `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC rather than a widened policy: widening `conversations_update` lets an admin bypass the trigger's gating entirely, which is the bigger hole.
  - **`p_resume` defaults to FALSE, and that is a product decision, not a default that fell out.** A verdict closes the review and leaves the thread locked unless the reviewer ticks "reopen for the other party". Safe either way — `messages_insert` independently requires the *sender's* account to be active, so reopening cannot undo a suspension — but it is the reviewer's call, so it is asked. `'kept_locked'` never resumes regardless. The UPDATE is guarded on `status = 'pending'`, so a second admin gets `P0002` rather than overwriting the first decision and its `reviewed_by`.
  - **Two of the three seeded flag patterns were dead on arrival, and the CHECK constraint could not have caught it.** Postgres regexes are POSIX ARE, where **`\b` is a backspace character**, not a word boundary — that is `\y`. `select 'call me on 9876543210' ~* '[6-9]\d{9}\b'` returns **false**; with `\y` it returns true. A `\b` pattern passes `flag_patterns_pattern_valid` (which only proves it compiles), saves cleanly, and silently never fires. Verified against the live DB before and after correcting. The seed migration now **asserts its own patterns match a probe string and do not match ordinary trade talk** ("...120 gsm combed cotton, signalling a repeat order" — which `\y` correctly ignores and a looser alternation would have flagged).
  - **The obvious client-side validation would have made it worse.** `new RegExp(pattern)` is ECMA-262: it **throws** on `(\+?91[\-\s]?)?[6-9]\d{9}\y` and **silently accepts** `\y(whats\s?app)\y` while reading `\y` as a literal "y". So a JS check would reject two of the three patterns this project ships and wave through the `\b` pattern that is dead in production — validating against the wrong language, which looks like it worked. Hence `regex_probe(pattern, sample)`, `SECURITY DEFINER` and role-gated, using the same `~*` the trigger uses. The admin editor now **blocks saving until a pattern has been shown to match a sample**, and a valid-but-non-matching result names `\b` as the likely cause.
  - **`keyword_blocklist` was deliberately left empty.** A blocklist hit is a hard stop with **zero review trail**: no message row, no lock, nothing queued, so support cannot see what was blocked or that anything was. Seeding it speculatively means silently rejecting legitimate messages with no way to find out. `chat_block_reasons` (7 rows) and `flag_patterns` (3) are seeded; the blocklist waits for a human to name a term.
  - **Moderation stopped being silent.** New `notifications` table, written **only** from inside the existing `SECURITY DEFINER` functions — no insert policy for any role, and `notify()` has no EXECUTE grant for any role. Both verified refused with a real login (42501 on the insert, "permission denied for function notify" on the RPC). A client that could write here could forge *"Your account has been reinstated"*.
  - **The non-disclosure rule shaped the schema, not just the copy.** `reason_id` is deliberately **not** a column on `notifications`: there would be no safe way to render it. The flag notice never names the matched pattern (that is a map for evading it), and `submit_report()`'s notice is **identical** to a pattern-triggered lock and goes only to the non-reporting participant — saying a report happened identifies the reporter by elimination in a two-person thread.
  - **`/notifications` was 100% mock and is now real, with the fake half made dev-only.** The store seeded 12 rows across 8 types into localStorage; the table backs 4 moderation kinds and nothing writes the other 7. Rather than leave fabricated business events in production, `devOnlySampleNotifications()` returns `[]` in a production build and is tree-shaken out — same rule as `devOnlyVideoCloseUps()`. **Production `/notifications` is therefore mostly empty today and the requirements spotlight will not fire. That is accurate, not a regression.** The store keeps the view model; read/dismiss now go to the database, so localStorage would only be a second, disagreeing copy. `KIND_META` is a lookup with a fallback, never an exhaustive `Record`, because `kind` is un-CHECKed on purpose. Loading, error and empty are three distinct states — the exact failure recorded for My Reviews.
  - **Suspension now blocks content creation** (`account_is_active()` ANDed onto eight insert policies). `product_videos` needed splitting: `pvideos_write` is FOR ALL, so gating it would also have stopped a suspended vendor **deleting their own videos**, which is not content creation. Split into insert/update/delete with the gate on INSERT only. **It does not take down live content, does not stop a running ad campaign** (ads activate via UPDATE after payment; `advertisements_insert` even requires `status <> 'active'`) **and does not end a session** — all stated in the migration header and asserted in the test.
  - **The active/suspended pairing in the gate test earned its keep immediately.** The ad case reported DENY while suspended and looked like a pass — but the ACTIVE half also reported DENY: the demo vendor's gold plan had lapsed on 2026-08-16, so `enforce_plan_limits` was raising `P0001` from a BEFORE trigger long before RLS was consulted. Testing only the suspended half would have recorded a passing gate that had never been exercised. The script now extends the billing period for the run and restores the exact original value.
  - **R-18 checked rather than assumed, and it has two different answers.** In the UI it **holds**: `VendorProfile` branches on `!user` first and the Website Address row is gated on the same resolved `contactVisible`, so a signed-out visitor is painted no phone, email, address or website. As **data** it does not: `vprofiles_select` is `USING (true)`, so the anon key reads `vendor_profiles.phone` straight from PostgREST — confirmed, real numbers, no session. Recorded as INFO rather than asserted away; closing it is a marketplace-discovery decision (vendor listing depends on that table being public), not something to slip into a moderation PR. **Flagged for a decision, not changed.**
  - **Verification, all against the live project with the DB returned to baseline afterwards:** `resolve_conversation_review` end-to-end 16/16 (lock, queue, keep-message, notify both, kept_locked ignores p_resume, resumed honours it, double-resolve `P0002`, invalid verdict `22023`); `notifications-check.mjs` 6/6; `suspension-gate-check.mjs` 4/4 paired; `contact-gate-check.mjs` 7/7 including the caller-beats-target ordering; Cosora-Admin's `chat-moderation-behaviour.mjs` 16/16. Final state confirmed: 0 suspended profiles, 0 ledger rows, 0 notifications, 0 reviews, 0 locked conversations, 3 flag patterns, 7 block reasons, 0 blocklist terms.
  - `tsc` **0 errors** (down from the long-standing 23 — the regenerated `database.types.ts` is what closed them), eslint clean on the changed files, `vite build` passes.
- 2026-08-02: **Chat moderation 2.5d — the last contact leak closed, and the gate stopped putting words in each surface's mouth.** `src/lib/queries/calls.ts`, `src/pages/VendorProfile.tsx`.
  - **Website Address was leaking from the other card.** `/vendor/:id` renders vendor data in two places, and 2.5c only gated one: "Detailed information" (expanded by default) still showed **Website Address**, a link straight off-platform, while Contact Details sat blocked right above it. That row is now gated too. **Company MD, Business Type, GST, PAN and Capacity deliberately stay visible** — those are registry/identity facts, not a way to reach anyone, and hiding them would make a moderated vendor look deleted rather than paused.
  - **One gate resolution, two consumers.** `contactVisible` is computed once from the already-resolved `useContactGate()` and read by both the card and the website row — the page does not run the gate twice. Unresolved counts as not-visible, so the website row cannot flash in and be taken back either.
  - **`callGate()` now returns a reason, not a sentence.** It reports `'caller_suspended' | 'target_suspended' | 'under_review'`, and two copy tables in `calls.ts` turn that into words: one for the call toast, one for a contact card. Previously a single string had to serve both, which is why a contact card was telling people *"calling is paused"* when nothing was being dialled — it now says *"This chat is under review — Contact details aren't available right now."*
  - `useContactGate()` resolves the contact-context copy itself and hands back `{title, description}`, so `VendorProfile` stays a dumb renderer that never learns the reason codes exist. The wording for a reason lives in exactly one place instead of drifting across every page that later grows a contact card.
  - **Verified the refactor changed nothing it shouldn't have.** All three toasts were captured from the real Call Now button afterwards and match the pre-refactor strings verbatim: *"Calling is unavailable / Your account is suspended…"*, *"Calling is unavailable / This account is currently suspended."*, *"This chat is under review — calling is paused / Our team will follow up."*
  - **All four 2.5c checks re-run against the live DB**, each state set in SQL and restored: signed out, both-active, target suspended, conversation under review. In every blocked case phone, email **and website** are absent from the whole page while all five registry rows still render; in the clear case everything returns, Website Address included.
  - `tsc` unchanged (same 23 pre-existing errors, none in the changed files), eslint clean, `vite build` passes.

The same card also rendered in full to **signed-out visitors**, with no account check of any kind.
  - `callGate()` and `CallBlock` are now **exported** rather than module-private, and a paired `useContactGate(otherId)` hook wraps them as reactive state. Same three checks, same wording, one rule — a surface that needs to decide what to *render* consults exactly what the click handler consults. Built with React Query to match `useCalls()` in the same file, and `staleTime: 0` because a lock applied a minute ago must be respected now, not served from a previous mount's cache.
  - **`loading` is load-bearing, not cosmetic.** Rendering the real rows "just until the check resolves" would flash the number and leak it anyway, so the card shows a skeleton until the gate resolves. **Verified by injecting a 6s delay on the gate's own request** and polling the DOM 86 times across the window: skeleton up at 121ms, the number never appeared once.

  - **The Report modal now sends the reason.** `submitReport()` takes a third argument and forwards it to `submit_report`'s `p_reported_reason`. Verified through the real UI, not just SQL: clicking *"Bullying or unwanted contact"* produced a `user_report` row holding exactly that string, while the `regex_flag` row beside it correctly kept `reported_reason = null` — nobody picked anything there.
  - **Calling was a hole straight through the chat lock.** `VendorProfile` has two "Call Now" buttons and `useCallVendor` has **17 call sites** app-wide; any of them could ring a suspended account or a party whose thread was locked. Fixed once inside the hook, not per call site — the next surface to add a call button inherits the gate instead of silently missing it.
  - Three checks, both directions (`useCallVendor` and `useCallBuyer`): caller's own `account_status`, target's `account_status`, and the existing conversation's `status`. The conversation lookup is a **plain SELECT on the sorted pair, deliberately not `ensureConversation()`** — that would CREATE a row as a side effect of merely asking whether a call is allowed.
  - **The gate runs before the phone lookup**, so a blocked attempt never reads the number, never logs a `calls` row, and never takes the "no phone on file → open chat" fallback. That fallback is untouched for calls that pass.
  - **`strict: false` bites discriminated unions.** The gate first returned `{ ok: true } | { ok: false; title }`; with `strictNullChecks` off TypeScript will not narrow a boolean-literal discriminant, so `if (!gate.ok)` left the union unnarrowed and every field access errored. Rewritten to return a nullable object (`null` = go ahead), which needs no narrowing. Worth remembering before reaching for that pattern again in this repo.
  - **The realtime push works, but is no longer trusted alone.** `conversations` joined the publication in 2.5a and the client now subscribes to its UPDATEs, so an admin resuming a thread unlocks both UIs live — something the old pull could never see, because no message accompanies a resume. Measured, however, that a self-inflicted lock (you send the message that trips the regex) can outrun the push, so the one-row pull on incoming messages is **kept as a backstop**, guarded to run only while still unlocked.
  - **A hypothesis tested and rejected rather than assumed:** the first failure looked like the classic "Realtime needs `REPLICA IDENTITY FULL`". Set FULL, retested — still failed; dropped to a direct `@supabase/supabase-js` subscription and the event arrived fine, proving the fault was my test harness racing the flip, not the DB. Retested DEFAULT with a properly armed probe: **event delivered, banner appeared with no reload.** `payload.new` is the complete new tuple regardless — replica identity only governs the OLD tuple. Left at DEFAULT, so the table matches migration `20260801154739` exactly and the WAL cost reasoned about there still stands.

All test data removed; DB confirmed back to 2 messages, zero moderation rows, `replica identity = default`.

  - **The actual bug: `VendorProfile` submitted the route param, not the vendor.** `reviews.vendor_id` is a `uuid` FK to `vendor_profiles(id)`, but the page passed `vendorId = id ?? "1"` — the raw URL segment. Buyer-side vendor links are **slugs** (`Following.tsx:88` → `/vendor/${brand.id}` where `followingStore` ids are `prezel`, `maison-lyra`, …), so the insert died on the cast — verified directly: `select 'prezel'::uuid` → `22P02 invalid input syntax for type uuid`. The buyer got an error toast and the review never existed, which is exactly the reported "I wrote a review and it isn't in My Reviews". Now submits `vendor.id` from `useVendorProfile`, and the CTA is **hidden** when there is no real vendor row rather than offering an action whose insert can only fail.
  - **The backend was never broken.** Confirmed against the live project: all three tables RLS-on with 4 correct policies each (public SELECT, `buyer_id = auth.uid()` for writes), all three upsert unique constraints present, and `invalidate()`'s `["reviews"]` prefix already covering `["reviews","mine",uid]`. The failure was entirely in the write paths and in the page's inability to show one.

Now four separate states: sign-in prompt, skeletons, an error state with Retry, and the genuine empty state.

Added, mirroring the service-vendor page. Read and write both key off `String(photographer.id)`, not the route param, so the two can't drift.
  - **Service vendor + freelancer reviews were already correct** (`service_reviews.service_id` is `text` with no FK, and `service_name` was passed). The 0 rows meant untested, not broken.
  - **Review photos now actually get written.** `product_reviews.photos` was read at `reviews.ts:120` and rendered at `ProductDetail.tsx:215` but **nothing ever wrote it**. Uploads reuse the **`product-images` bucket** under `${buyerId}/reviews/…`, exactly as `rfqs.ts:193` already does for buyer RFQ images — its insert policy is uploader-scoped (`(storage.foldername(name))[1] = auth.uid()::text`), verified from `pg_policies`, and the bucket is public so everyone reads them back. Capped at **4 photos / 2 MB each**, deliberately: Free-plan egress is 5 GB/month shared org-wide and review photos are re-read on every product page view.
  - **`undefined` photos leaves stored photos alone; `[]` clears them.** Built by conditional spread rather than a `Record<string, unknown>` payload — the latter typechecks locally but defeats Supabase's generated Insert/Update types and added 2 `tsc` errors before being fixed.
  - **Four duplicated `WriteReviewModal` copies collapsed into one**, plus a shared `StarRating`/`StarPicker` replacing five star implementations that had drifted into two palettes (amber `#f5a623` on My Reviews vs. `yellow-400` in the modals). A shared `ReviewCard` was **deliberately not** built — the five review sections are legitimately different designs, so unifying them would have been a cosmetic refactor with real regression risk and no functional gain.
  - **My Reviews no longer invents images.** A product that goes non-live returns a null join under `products_select` RLS, and the page used to fall back to a **random picsum photo** — a real review of a real product shown beside an unrelated stock image. Now: neutral icon tile, "Product no longer available", link suppressed. Cards also deep-link to the reviewed entity (`vendor_id`/`product_id`/`service_kind+service_id` added to the selects), photographer resolving to `/cosora-studio/:id`.

- 2026-08-01: **Chat moderation 2.5a — the reporter's reason is no longer thrown away, and the lock is now pushable.** New `supabase/migrations/20260801154739_report_reason_and_conversations_realtime.sql`; `src/lib/database.types.ts` regenerated; `CLAUDE.md`. Schema only — the UI still sends the two-argument call until 2.5b.
  - **`conversation_reviews.reported_reason text null`.** The Report modal has always asked "Why are you reporting this?" and then discarded the answer: support saw a pending review with no idea what was actually complained about. Kept as **free text with no CHECK** — the seven `REPORT_REASONS` strings are UI copy that product will reword, and constraining schema to UI copy turns a wording change into a migration.
  - **It is deliberately NOT `reason_id`.** That column is an FK into the admin-curated `chat_block_reasons` — the reviewer's *verdict*. `reported_reason` is the reporter's *claim*. Collapsing the two would destroy exactly the signal a moderation queue exists to surface: where the reporter and the reviewer disagreed.
  - **`submit_report()` had to be DROPPED and recreated, not `CREATE OR REPLACE`d.** Adding a third parameter with a DEFAULT creates an **overload**, not a replacement — and the app's existing two-argument call would then have failed with *"function is not unique"*, breaking every report in production the moment this shipped. Verified afterwards from `pg_proc` that exactly **one** signature exists, `submit_report(uuid,uuid,text)`. Dropping also drops the grants, so EXECUTE was re-granted (confirmed via `has_function_privilege`).
  - **Back-compat proven, not assumed.** A rollback-safe `DO` block impersonated the buyer via `set_config('request.jwt.claims', …)` so `auth.uid()`/`is_conversation_member()` behaved as they do for a real request, then called the function **both ways**: 2 rows written, `with_reason=1` (3-arg) and `null_reason=1` (2-arg, default applied). So the un-updated client keeps working in the window before 2.5b.
  - **`conversations` added to the `supabase_realtime` publication.** Previously only `messages` was published, so a lock reached the client only by re-reading the row when a message happened to arrive — and an admin *resuming* a thread was invisible, since no message accompanies it.
  - **REPLICA IDENTITY left at DEFAULT on purpose.** Realtime filters and RLS-checks `postgres_changes` UPDATEs against the NEW record, which already carries `id` and `status`. FULL would write every column of every `conversations` UPDATE to the WAL — and `bump_conversation()` fires one on **every message sent** — which on a Free-plan project is real egress for a `payload.old` nothing reads.
  - Verified from `pg_catalog`/`information_schema` rather than the migration text: column present and nullable, single function signature with `DEFAULT NULL::text`, still `SECURITY DEFINER`, and `pg_publication_tables` now lists `conversations, messages`. DB left clean — 0 review rows, 0 locked conversations.
- 2026-08-01: **Chat moderation Phase 2 — the buyer/vendor UI wired to the Phase 1 backend.** `src/lib/queries/chat.ts`, `src/pages/ChatThread.tsx`. No routing changes; `App.tsx` untouched.
  - **`sendText()` now reports failure instead of swallowing it.** It returns a boolean and `send()` only clears the draft when the row actually landed — losing what someone typed on the one path where they need to edit it was the old behaviour. Three distinct messages: the blocklist RAISE (matched on its text, which PostgREST passes through verbatim), an RLS `42501` (which covers *both* a locked thread and a suspended sender — they are indistinguishable from the client, so the copy names both), and anything else.
  - **Nothing was ever optimistically appended, and that is what makes this safe.** The thread renders only what the realtime echo returns, so a rejected message is structurally incapable of appearing as sent. The requirement was already satisfied by the existing design; the change is purely about telling the user.
  - **The lock is pulled, not pushed — `conversations` is not in the `supabase_realtime` publication (only `messages` is).** Rather than a DDL change in a UI phase, `refreshStatus()` re-reads the one row on the events that can change it: any message arriving (either side's message can trip the regex trigger) and a successful report. It short-circuits once already locked, since only an admin can unlock and that is not observable here either way. **Known limit: an admin resuming a thread is not seen until remount.**
  - **`under_review` disables the composer, hides the call button, and shows an undismissable banner** — identical for buyer and seller, because the lock is on the thread, not on a role. The banner sits inside the sticky header band so it cannot be scrolled away. The call button is *hidden* rather than greyed: `useCallVendor()` reveals the other party's real phone number, and a dead affordance invites retrying.
  - **A disabled control that still looked enabled.** The send/mic buttons are `motion.button`, and framer writes `opacity` as an **inline style**, which beats Tailwind's `disabled:opacity-40` class. They were genuinely disabled and looked completely normal. Opacity is now driven through `animate` instead. Caught in a screenshot, not by a test assertion — `isDisabled()` was already passing.
  - **ReportModal now calls `submit_report()` for real.** The confirmation screen is reached only after the RPC resolves; a failure shows an inline error and leaves the reason list up to retry. The reason list stays UI-only — `submit_report` takes no reason, because `conversation_reviews.reason_id` points at the admin-curated `chat_block_reasons` table, not these seven strings. **Nothing captures which reason the user picked; if that matters it needs a schema change.**
  - **Verified against the real app and the real database** — dev server, real demo accounts, real RLS, no mocks. Seeded a realistic blocklist term (`whatsapp`) and flag pattern (an Indian mobile number) as real rows, then deleted them and every trace afterwards. **Blocklisted message: toast shown, thread count unchanged, draft retained, and `0` rows persisted confirmed by SQL.** **Flagged message: the message IS kept (support has to read it), conversation flipped to `under_review`, exactly `1` `regex_flag` review row naming the right pattern and the right message.** Banner + disabled composer + hidden call button confirmed on **both** the buyer and the vendor view of the same thread. Report flow filed a `user_report` row against the newest message and locked the thread in place with no reload; the failure branch was exercised by forcing the RPC to 500. DB returned to baseline: 2 messages, zero moderation rows anywhere.
  - `tsc` unchanged at the same 23 pre-existing errors, eslint clean on both changed files, `vite build` passes.
  - **"Block Vendor" deliberately untouched** — still the fake self-service action, per the brief.
- 2026-08-01: **Chat moderation Phase 1 — backend only: admin visibility, a hard keyword blocklist, regex auto-flagging that locks a thread, and account suspension unified onto `profiles`.** New `supabase/migrations/20260801095820_chat_moderation_and_account_suspension.sql`; `src/lib/database.types.ts` regenerated. **No UI files touched — that is Phase 2.**
  - **Four mechanisms, deliberately not merged.** `keyword_blocklist` is a HARD stop (BEFORE INSERT raises, no row written, nothing flagged — a blocklist rejection is *not* a moderation event). `flag_patterns` is a SOFT stop (AFTER INSERT — the message **is** kept, because support needs to read it; the conversation flips to `under_review` and a `conversation_reviews` row queues it). `submit_report()` is the same lock, participant-initiated. `account_suspensions` is the outcome ledger, separate from the live flag.
  - **Step 1's data check, done before writing a line of DDL:** all 7 rows of `vendor_profiles.account_status` held `'active'` — which *is* the column default (`text not null default 'active'`). No view depended on it and nothing outside `database.types.ts` ever read it. So no backfill, and the column was dropped. Suspension moved to `profiles.account_status` because it is an **account-level** fact: the same human toggles between buyer and vendor, so a vendor-table flag cannot stop them messaging as a buyer.
  - **Dropping that column would have broken vendor writes.** `enforce_vendor_profile_admin_fields()` (from `20260717130000`) reads `new.account_status`; a plpgsql body is not validated against the column until it runs, so this would have surfaced as every vendor-profile write failing at runtime, not as a migration error. The function was rewritten to gate `is_verified` only, *before* the drop.
  - **Two self-service bypasses found in the live policies and closed.** `profiles_update` allows `id = auth.uid()`, so a suspended user could have PATCHed `account_status` back to `'active'`; `conversations_update` allows either participant, so a flagged user could have PATCHed `status` back to `'active'` and un-locked the thread they were just locked out of. **A lock any participant can unlock is not a lock.** Both are now column-level gates in BEFORE triggers — the pattern `20260717130000` already established, because RLS cannot see *which* column changed. `enforce_admin_grants()` gained an `account_status` clause; new `enforce_conversation_status()` blocks participants but allows support/super_admin (so a future admin panel can resume a thread). Both `SECURITY DEFINER` callers bypass via the `current_user <> 'authenticated'` escape hatch.
  - **`check_message_blocklist()` had to be SECURITY DEFINER, and that is load-bearing, not stylistic.** `keyword_blocklist` is admin-only under RLS, so a sender's own trigger would read zero rows and **the blocklist would silently never fire**. Matching uses `strpos(lower(...))` rather than `ILIKE`: a term containing `%` or `_` would otherwise be a LIKE wildcard and match far more than the admin intended. Empty terms are skipped (`strpos` returns 1 for `''`, which would block every message).
  - **One malformed regex in `flag_patterns` would have taken chat down platform-wide** — it raises inside an AFTER INSERT trigger on every message. Added `check (('' ~ pattern) is not null)`; `~` is immutable so it is a legal CHECK, and it fails the bad admin write instead of the messages. Verified: `'bad[(regex'` is rejected with *"invalid regular expression: brackets [] not balanced"*.
  - **The send gate is server-side truth, not UI state.** `messages_insert` now additionally requires the conversation's `status = 'active'` AND the sender's own `profiles.account_status = 'active'`, ANDed onto the existing check rather than replacing it — so a locked thread and a suspended sender are unsendable even with the client bypassed entirely. `conversations`/`messages` SELECT gained an OR for `is_admin() AND admin_role() IN ('support','super_admin')`.
  - **Verified with a live rollback-safe smoke test** (one DO block, ending in a RAISE so the whole thing aborts — zero rows persisted, confirmed by a follow-up count on all five tables): blocklisted message **rejected** with `message blocked: contains a restricted term` and **0 rows persisted**; a message matching an active pattern left the conversation at **`under_review`** with **1** `regex_flag` review row.
  - New tables `chat_block_reasons`, `keyword_blocklist`, `flag_patterns`, `conversation_reviews`, `account_suspensions` (all RLS-enabled, support/super_admin gated; `account_suspensions` has **no** write policy for any role — it is written only by `set_account_status()`). New RPCs `set_account_status()` and `submit_report()`, both `SECURITY DEFINER`, styled on `grant_ad_verification()`/`reply_to_review()`.
  - **`tsc` error count unchanged at 23 before and after the types regen** — diffed both lists; the only differences are three message strings that now name `profiles.account_status` and no longer name `vendor_profiles.account_status`, which is itself confirmation both halves landed. All 23 are pre-existing and unrelated. Supabase advisors: no ERROR-level findings, no RLS gaps.
- 2026-08-01: **Video Closeups — adversarial audit, then the fixes it justified: moderation was bypassable three ways, a resumed upload wrote to a dead path, and nothing could be rejected.** New migrations `20260801085830_enforce_product_videos_moderation.sql`, `20260801100223_add_product_video_reject_path.sql`, `20260801100327_revoke_public_execute_on_reject_vendor_content.sql`, `20260801102505_approve_vendor_content_per_item.sql`; `src/lib/queries/videos.ts`, `src/pages/UploadVideo.tsx`, `src/pages/VideoCloseUpsPage.tsx`, `src/lib/database.types.ts` regenerated.
  - **Vendors could publish straight to the buyer feed, three ways** — proven with a real vendor JWT against the live DB, not inferred from code: `INSERT` with `status:'live'` → 201 stored live; `INSERT` omitting status → 201 stored live; `PATCH under_review → live` → 200 stored live. Two independent causes, both fixed. (1) **`product_videos.status` defaulted to `'live'`** — the only moderated table that did, `products` and `catalogues` both default to `under_review`. A vendor did not even have to try, and the *only* thing holding moderation together was `createProductVideo()` sending `status:'under_review'` from the client, which is not a security boundary. (2) **`product_videos` had no triggers at all** while `products` has `trg_products_moderation`. RLS `pvideos_write` only constrains `vendor_id`; *which transitions are legal* belongs in a BEFORE trigger, which is where `products` already put it. Added `enforce_product_videos_moderation()` modelled on the products one. All three attacks now neutralised (`42501` on the PATCH), and a vendor can still withdraw to draft and resubmit.
  - **A resumed upload wrote its bytes to one path and recorded another.** `tus-js-client` fingerprints an upload as `tus-br-{name}-{type}-{size}-{lastModified}-{endpoint}` — **no `objectName`** — so when a vendor reloaded mid-upload and resubmitted, `createProductVideo()` minted a fresh random path while `findPreviousUploads()` matched the old session, already bound server-side to the old object. Reproduced with a 30MB fixture: the row's `video_url` pointed at **nothing** (a 404 for buyers, after passing moderation) and the fully-uploaded 30,704,510-byte object was referenced by no row — invisible to `deleteProductVideo()`, which only removes URLs a row points at, so it stayed billed forever against a 5GB/month plan. `resumableUpload()` now resolves the path *before* uploading, adopts the pending session's `objectName` when there is one, and **returns the path the bytes actually went to**; the caller persists that, never its own proposal. Adoption is guarded on the folder prefix so a shared browser cannot resume into another vendor's folder (storage RLS would reject it, turning a clean retry into an opaque failure). The poster path is derived from the winning video path too, so a retry overwrites its own poster instead of stranding one. Same repro re-run: `video_bytes = 30704510`, 2 objects, **zero orphans**.
  - **There was no reject path at all.** `reject_vendor_content` 404'd (`PGRST202`) — yet `product_status` has had a `rejected` value all along, `MyVideoRow` types it and `UploadVideo` renders it. A reel that should never go live could only be left pending forever or deleted outright, with no reason recorded and nothing to show the vendor. Added `product_videos.rejection_reason` (matching `products`' column exactly) and `reject_vendor_content(target_table, target_id, reason)`: same authz gate as approve, scoped to **one row**, `target_table` checked against a fixed list with a static UPDATE per branch — no dynamic SQL, which inside a `SECURITY DEFINER` function would be an injection vector. Rejection may act from *any* status, so a moderator can also take down something already live.
  - **`rejection_reason` needed trigger protection, not just a column.** `pvideos_write` constrains `vendor_id` and nothing else, so adding the column alone would have handed vendors write access to the moderator's note on their own row — the same class of hole just closed. The two `rejection_reason` clauses from `enforce_products_moderation()` were carried across: nulled on insert for non-moderators, immutable on update. Verified: vendor edit → `42501`; vendor pre-seeding a reason on INSERT → stored `null`.
  - **Approve became per-item, mirroring reject.** Reject was per-row but approve was per-*vendor*: a moderator could reject one bad clip but could not approve one good clip without also publishing everything else that vendor had pending, across products AND videos AND catalogues in a single call. `approve_vendor_content(uuid)` was **renamed** (`ALTER FUNCTION`, not recreated) to `approve_vendor_content_bulk` — OID `26164`, body md5 and length all carried over unchanged — and a new `approve_vendor_content(target_table, target_id)` mirrors reject's shape. **One deliberate asymmetry:** approve is gated on `status = 'under_review'`, so approving a draft or an already-live row **raises `P0002`** rather than reporting success while silently doing nothing. Reject gets the wider latitude; approve does not. Safe to rename because the old function had zero callers — verified in `src/` (present only in the generated types file), in `supabase/functions/`, and against `pg_proc.prosrc` for DB-internal references.
  - **The 60s duration cap failed open.** `handleFileSelect` set `selectedFile` *before* `probeVideoFile()` and then returned early on a null probe, leaving the file selected and submittable with the `MAX_VIDEO_SECONDS` check skipped entirely and only the 50MB byte cap left — at a low bitrate, many minutes of video. An unverifiable clip now shows an in-form warning and is blocked at submit (chosen over silently dropping the file, so the vendor can see what happened and re-export). Found adjacent while in there: **`probe` was never cleared between files**, so picking a good clip and then a bad one left the previous file's probe in state — the duration check passed on the old numbers and those numbers were persisted against the new upload.
  - **Dev sample clips latched permanently into the buyer reel.** `devOnlyVideoCloseUps()` returns its samples *synchronously* while the real query is still pending, so `VideoCloseUpsPage`'s `openList` snapshot froze those and never re-synced — in dev the reel showed sample clips even with real rows in `product_videos`, which makes hand-testing this feature actively misleading. Production was never affected (the helper returns `[]` there) — confirmed empirically against a `vite preview` build rather than assumed. The snapshot now records which source it came from and re-arms exactly once, when real rows supersede the fallback; the freeze it exists for — bookmarking must not reorder the reel mid-scroll — is kept.
  - **What the audit proved was already working**, each measured rather than read: the bucket's `file_size_limit` rejects a 53,000,000-byte direct POST (`413 EntityTooLarge`) and the TUS path refuses it *at creation* instead of after reading 65s of body; `allowed_mime_types` rejects `video/quicktime` (`415`); a 654s clip is rejected client-side before any storage request; cross-vendor RLS holds both ways (spoofed `vendor_id` → `403`, another vendor's `under_review` invisible to vendor, buyer and anon alike); TUS genuinely resumes after a mid-chunk `ERR_CONNECTION_RESET` (`HEAD` handshake, then `PATCH` at the same non-zero offset, one create for the session); `FEED_LIMIT` returns exactly 30 of 35 ordered by `views_count desc`; the viewer holds at **peak 3 `<video>` / 10 `<img>` across 30 real slides**; VendorProfile shows only its own vendor's clips, and Call Now dials the correct number for two different vendors.
  - **Known gaps, deliberately out of scope.** The MIME allowlist keys off the *declared* content-type only — a `.mov` renamed and declared `video/mp4` is accepted (HTTP 200) — and the real fix is content sniffing or transcoding, i.e. new infrastructure. And **`views_count` is dead data**: nothing in the codebase writes it (there is an `increment_product_view` RPC for products, no video equivalent), yet it is the SQL sort key applied *before* client-side ranking runs. So every real upload starts and stays at 0, the top-30 tiebreak is arbitrary, and a video ranked 31st can never surface however well it matches a buyer. Likes and saves are local-only too — `saved_items`/`saved_folder_items` stay empty while *product* saves write to them, so a buyer who saves a video loses it on refresh while a saved product survives. Whether to build real counters is a product decision, not a patch.
  - `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json` at the **same 23 pre-existing errors**, none of them in any changed file; eslint clean; `vite build` passes. All four migration files match their DB-recorded statements (normalised md5, compared both ways). DB restored to baseline after every test — **0 video rows, 0 storage objects**, 29 products all live, buyer preferences untouched.

Four buyer surfaces then fell back to seven *more* hardcoded clips, so the feed looked populated regardless. Sintel carried the highest `views_count`, so it sorted into slot 0 and eagerly preloaded — making any performance measurement of the feed meaningless.

Exceeding it 402s the whole app, not just video. The old 200 MB client-only cap meant one file could burn 2.5% of the monthly budget per view.

The mock fallback now goes through `devOnlyVideoCloseUps()`, which returns `[]` unless `import.meta.env.DEV` — **proven tree-shaken, the sample URLs do not appear in the production bundle**. Rails omit themselves when empty. `/video-closeups` was a **blank white screen with no way out** when the catalogue was empty (`VideoCloseUpsViewer` returns null on an empty list) — it now has a real empty state and a working back button. Sample clips replaced with 9:16 portrait, dev-only.
  - **`VendorProfile.tsx:299` was a correctness bug, not a cosmetic one:** a real vendor with no videos rendered seven *other* brands' clips inside their profile, and "Call Now" then fired `callVendor()` with a mock `vendorId` (`"v5"`) — content misattributed to a named business plus a wrong-supplier dial. Now their own videos or nothing.
  - **Phase 1 — data layer.** `fetchVideoCloseUps` was two round-trips (rows, then `vendor_profiles` joined in memory) because the only FK pointed at `profiles`. Added a second FK to `vendor_profiles` and switched to a PostgREST embed — **verified with a live REST call returning `vendor_profiles: {brand_name: "Tirupur Textiles"}` in one request**. A FK rather than denormalising `brand_name`, which would have created a permanent sync obligation. Plus `.limit(30)`/`.limit(24)`, a partial index on `(views_count desc) where status='live'`, and per-query cache settings. **The highest-leverage single change: `App.tsx` was a bare `new QueryClient()`**, so every query app-wide had `staleTime: 0` and refetched on every mount and tab focus; it now defaults to 60s / no refetch-on-focus.
  - **Phase 2 — the viewer, where the load-time win is.** Windowed via spacer shells: every index always renders its wrapper `<div>`, only the contents are windowed, so scroll height, snap positions and `slideRefs[i]` stay correct with no measurement code and **the IntersectionObserver effect never re-runs**. `preload` is now by distance from the active slide — it read `Math.abs(index) <= 1`, the *absolute* slide number, so slides 0 and 1 downloaded eagerly on open and every slide from 2 on was permanently `preload="none"`, stalling the moment it was scrolled to. Poster renders as a sibling underlay with the video fading in over it (`onLoadedData`), so there is never a black rectangle. `currentTime = 0` is guarded on `readyState`. On unmount: `pause()` → `removeAttribute("src")` → `load()` — **not `src=""`**, which resolves against the document URL and makes the browser fetch the page and try to decode it as media. Reel order is frozen when the viewer opens, because bookmarking re-ran the ranking and reshuffled the list under the user's finger.
  - **Measured with a 30-item catalogue on the production build: peak 3 `<video>` and 10 `<img>` in the DOM, versus 30 and 60 unwindowed**, 25–27 empty shells, media fetched incrementally (2 on open → 17 after eight slides). *Correction to the plan, which claimed ≤2 videos: the real bound is 3 — `distance <= 1` means previous + active + next. Still bounded independent of list length.*
  - **Phase 3 — upload hardening.** Real **TUS resumable upload** via `tus-js-client` (6 MB chunks, direct `<ref>.storage.supabase.co` hostname, `findPreviousUploads`/`resumeFromPreviousUpload`, `removeFingerprintOnSuccess`), with chunk-level progress in the UI. Client-side probe reads duration/dimensions and generates the poster **from the video file itself** via canvas — which is what makes the poster→video swap pixel-congruent instead of visibly re-cropping — timing out after 3s rather than blocking. Bucket now enforces **50 MB** and a MIME allowlist server-side. `video/quicktime` dropped: an iPhone's `.mov` is HEVC, which fails on Chrome/Android with no transcoding step to save it. `deleteProductVideo` removes storage objects **before** the row, so a failure leaves a recoverable row rather than an unrecoverable orphan.
  - **The MIME allowlist trap, handled:** posters are written to the *same* bucket, so a video-only allowlist would throw *after* the video object was already created — every upload would half-succeed with an orphan and no row. The image types are load-bearing.

Approved it, and the buyer reel played it from Supabase with **HTTP 206 Partial Content** (range streaming), `readyState 4`, faded in, real brand name and ₹499 price. The real win was already banked in Phase 3: `tus-js-client` (15.8 KB gzip) is its own chunk that buyers never download.

- 2026-07-28: **Brand Picks and Recommended Premium Brands now route to real products/vendors instead of placeholders.** `src/pages/NewArrivals.tsx`, `src/lib/queries/ads.ts`, `src/lib/queries/vendor.ts`; migrations `20260727185837_add_category_name_to_active_ads`, `20260727191504_make_active_ads_ordering_total`.
  - Both sections were static mock arrays. Every Brand Picks card linked to `/search/results` and every Premium Brands tile to `/vendor/premium-1`, regardless of which was tapped, and both used `placeCall(name, demoPhone(name))` — a synthetic number.
  - **Brand Picks** now reads `useActiveAds()`, the same paid-ad pipeline `SponsoredRail` uses. Cards route to `/product/${ad.productId}`, Call Now uses `useCallVendor(ad.vendorId, …)`, and impressions/clicks log through `logAdImpression`/`logAdClick` — previously nothing on this section was tracked at all. The existing visual design (image, name, category, price, Call Now) is unchanged; the old markup nested a `<button>` inside a `<Link>`, which is invalid, so the card body and Call Now are now siblings.
  - **Category label / RPC decision:** `ActiveAd` had no category, and reading `products`/`advertisements` from the buyer client would defeat the `active_ads` SECURITY DEFINER boundary that keeps `daily_budget` away from buyers. So the RPC was extended instead — one new trailing `category_name` column via a `categories` left join. Strictly additive: filters, joins and LIMIT unchanged, existing callers map by field name and ignore extra columns, so `SponsoredRail` is untouched. EXECUTE grants re-checked after the required DROP/CREATE (`anon`, `authenticated`, `service_role` all present).
  - **Premium Brands** now reads a new `useTopVendors()` in `vendor.ts` (Option A): all vendors with at least one live product, ranked active-paid-plan-first (`plan_expires_at > now()`, the same signal as `fetchOpenRfqs`' `isPaid` and `trustSealFromParts`), then rating, then review count. Tiles route to the real `/vendor/:id`. Cover image is the vendor's first live product photo; the tile shows brand, city, rating and live-product count. Option B (a real vendor-level paid placement) was **not** built, per the brief.
  - The old "AD" label on Premium Brands was dropped — there is no vendor-level ad product, so this is a merit ranking, not paid inventory, and labelling it AD would misrepresent it. Replaced with "Top-rated verified suppliers on Cosora".
  - **De-duplication with SponsoredRail:** the two surfaces now split one ordered window rather than both showing everything — `SPONSORED_MAX = 3` at the call site (the component is unmodified), Brand Picks renders rows 4+ of a 9-row fetch. Both sections hide entirely when their slice is empty, the same rule SponsoredRail already follows. **Consequence Mitra should weigh in on:** the sections split inventory, so Brand Picks stays hidden until more than 3 campaigns are live.
  - **Bug found and fixed while verifying the split:** the first browser pass showed "Linen Camp Shirt" in *both* rails. Root cause was not the slice but the RPC's ordering — `order by a.created_at desc` alone is not a total order, and ties are real in production data (the two seed ads share `2026-07-05 07:33:15.879125+00`; four rows inserted in one statement shared a timestamp exactly). Postgres returned ties in a different order per call, so different `max_count` windows overlapped and an ad could be impression-counted twice per page view. Second migration adds `a.id` as a tiebreaker. Proved in SQL (`overlapping_ads 0` between the 3-row and 4-row windows) and re-proved in the browser (Sponsored: Oversized Crew Tee, Floral Wrap Dress, Mesh Training Tee; Brand Picks: Linen Camp Shirt, Slim Fit Jeans, Oversized Graphic Tee, Premium Cotton Polo; overlap none).
  - **Live data caveat, important:** `active_ads` currently returns **zero rows** — all 3 campaigns in `advertisements` have `status='active'` but `ends_at` in the past (2026-07-14, 2026-07-17, 2026-07-23). So Brand Picks renders nothing today, and SponsoredRail has been invisible for the same reason. This is a data/campaign-lifecycle issue, not a wiring one; nothing in the app moves a lapsed campaign out of `status='active'`.
  - Verified live against real data by temporarily extending `ends_at` and adding 4 temporary campaigns. Brand Picks showed real products with real categories (`Linen Camp Shirt / Shirt / ₹689`, `Slim Fit Jeans / Jeans / ₹799`); clicking one navigated to `/product/e5860396-…` and the real product page loaded. Call Now fired `vendor_profiles?select=phone,brand_name&id=eq.22222222-…`, confirming the real `useCallVendor` path rather than `demoPhone`. Premium Brands rendered 4 real vendors with hrefs `/vendor/22222222-…`, `/vendor/6f66d05d-…`, `/vendor/a0000001-…`, `/vendor/a0000005-…`; clicking the first loaded that vendor's real storefront. No console errors, no HTTP >= 400.

  - Pre-checks before applying: (1) bucket list re-queried directly from `storage.buckets` — exactly 5, no unaudited bucket, and **all had 0 objects**, consistent with uploads never having worked; (2) nothing admin-facing reads `storage.objects` directly — there is no admin UI at all yet, every storage call in `src/` is `upload` + `getPublicUrl` (no `.list()`, `.download()`, `.createSignedUrl()`, `.remove()`), and product images are read from the stored `product_images.url` column. These buckets are public, so file serving already bypasses RLS; the policy only lets an uploader read back their own object metadata row, which is what `INSERT ... RETURNING` needs.

- 2026-07-26: **Storage upload failure — ROOT CAUSE FOUND. Every Supabase Storage upload in this app has been broken, not just the new feature. Fix proposed, NOT applied (awaiting approval).**
  - **Root cause: four of the five buckets have INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policies but no SELECT policy.** Supabase Storage's upload performs an `INSERT ... RETURNING` on `storage.objects`; under RLS, `INSERT ... RETURNING <columns>` also requires a **SELECT** policy that admits the new row. Without one Postgres raises `42501 new row violates row-level security policy`, which reads like the INSERT was refused when in fact the insert is fine and only the read-back is denied.
  - Affected: `avatars`, `catalogues`, `product-images`, `product-videos`. Unaffected: `business-docs`, the only bucket with a SELECT policy (`business_docs_owner_select`).
  - Evidence (all as role `authenticated` with JSON jwt claims, rolled back): `insert … into 'avatars' returning name` → **42501**; the byte-identical `insert … returning '<literal>'` → **OK**; `insert … into 'business-docs' returning name` → **OK**. The literal-only RETURNING needs no SELECT policy, which is why it passed and misled the first pass of this investigation.
  - Ruled out first, per instruction, before touching auth internals: the real failing request was captured from the browser rather than reconstructed. Object key `product-images/22222222-…/dedab252-…/0.png`, first folder `22222222-…` equals the session's `auth.uid()`, the `Authorization` header carried the user's own JWT with `sub` = same uid and `role: authenticated`. Path construction and auth context are both correct, so this was never a path-mismatch bug. `auth.uid()` is the standard definition and resolves correctly under both the flat (`request.jwt.claim.sub`) and JSON (`request.jwt.claims`) conventions.
  - **Correction to the previous entry:** the earlier claim that "`with_check` evaluates true yet the INSERT is still rejected" was an artifact of those probe statements using `RETURNING bucket_id, name`. The INSERT policy was never the problem.
  - **Proposed fix (not applied):** add an owner-scoped SELECT policy per affected bucket, mirroring the existing `business_docs_owner_select`, e.g. `create policy product_images_owner_select on storage.objects for select to authenticated using (bucket_id = 'product-images' and (storage.foldername(name))[1] = auth.uid()::text);`. These four buckets are `public: true`, so public file serving already bypasses RLS: this grants an uploader read access to their own object *metadata row* only, which is what upload needs, and does not widen access to the files themselves.
  - Scope note: this also explains why vendor product images, vendor catalogues, product videos and avatar uploads never persisted. Those are shipped features, so the fix is worth doing regardless of this stage.

The text description field is untouched and still required when customization is requested. `customizationFiles` is now always `[]`; `uploadCustomizationImages()` and the `customizationFiles` field in `rfqs.ts` are deliberately kept so re-enabling is a UI-only change once storage is fixed. Sheet motion matches `QuickRfqModal`/`ProductChatOptionsSheet`.

Actual rows: rfq `ecd5a47e-…` with `vendor_id a0000001-…`, `product_id b0000000-…-004`, `quantity 350`, `sizes_breakdown [{"size":"S","quantity":200},{"size":"M","quantity":150}]`, `colors ["Charcoal"]`, `budget 180.00–240.00`, `customization_requested true` + notes, `status active`; message `903234f4-…` `kind=quote_request`, `rfq_id` linked, in the conversation between buyer `11111111-…` and vendor `a0000001-…`.

Diagnosis so far: the bucket choice is right (`product_images_owner_insert` is uploader-scoped, `(storage.foldername(name))[1] = auth.uid()::text`, roles `{authenticated}`, and the bucket is public so vendors could read the URLs back); `authenticated` holds INSERT on `storage.objects`; all 16 policies are PERMISSIVE, none RESTRICTIVE; and in a simulated `authenticated` session for the exact path the `with_check` expression evaluates to **true** — yet `insert into storage.objects` is still rejected with `42501`. The same insert as `postgres` (RLS bypassed) succeeds, so constraints and triggers are fine and the blocker is purely RLS. Not resolved and not worked around: no second storage path, no base64 fallback, no new bucket. Consequence: attaching a reference image currently fails the whole request. **Awaiting a decision.**

- 2026-07-26: **Fixed: vendor product edits were silently discarded (pre-existing bug).** `src/pages/Upload.tsx` line ~271. `handleSubmit` opened with `if (category?.type !== "product") { toast.success(...); return; }`. Edit mode skips the category/sub-category steps, so `selectedCategory` was always null there, `category` was null, the guard tripped, and every edit returned before `updateProduct()` — showing a bogus **"Profile submitted! Your listing is now pending review."** toast while issuing no PATCH at all. Changed to `if (!isEdit && category?.type !== "product")`.
  - **Safety of the `!isEdit` narrowing, verified directly rather than assumed** — editing is products-only by construction: (1) `<Upload />` is mounted on exactly one route, `/upload` (App.tsx:230); (2) edit mode is keyed solely off `?id=`; (3) the only two links to `/upload?id=` are `Products.tsx:143` (VendorProductRow) and `SellerHome.tsx:264` (topProducts), both iterating product rows; (4) `fetchProductForEdit` queries `.from("products")` only; (5) **no services or freelancers table exists in the database** (`information_schema.tables` lists 31 tables, none of them service/freelancer listings) so no non-product listing can have an id to edit. The guard's original purpose is preserved for new listings.
  - Verified live (Playwright + network trace, real auth as Demo Vendor, seeded row `ee000000-…`, baseline `price 100 / sizes ["S"] / colour "Navy" / customization false`):
    - **Save as Draft** → `PATCH` fired, `200`, body `{"price_value":275,"sizes":["S","M","L"],"colour":"Navy","customization_available":true,"status":"draft"}`; toast **"Product updated / Saved as draft."**; DB read back `275.00 / ["S","M","L"] / Navy / true / draft`.
    - **Save Changes** (the primary CTA, a different path: form `onSubmit` rather than button `onClick`) → `PATCH` fired, `200`; toast **"Product updated / Resubmitted for review (24–48h)."**; DB `310.00 / under_review`.
    - **Regression check** — a *new* Service listing (Logistics & Supply Chain) still hits the guard: toast "Service submitted! Your Logistics & Supply Chain is now pending review." and zero product rows written (`STAGE1GUARD%` count = 0).
  - Toast copy needed no separate fix: with the guard bypassed, edits reach the existing correct `toast.success("Product updated", …)`. The "Profile submitted!" copy is now only reachable for new freelancer listings, where it is accurate.
  - Note: colour is **preserved** through an edit, not editable in it — edit mode renders no category dynamic fields, so there is no colour control. The PATCH body proves `colour` is genuinely carried in the update payload rather than dropped/nulled. Colour *changing* is covered by the insert-path test (Black+Tan → "Black").
  - Test rows deleted (`products` 29, `rfqs` 2). `tsc --noEmit --skipLibCheck` 0 errors, eslint clean, `vite build` passes.

Closes the gap flagged in the Stage 1 entry: `sellerCategories.ts` collected a `colors` field but the submit handler never mapped it, so every newly uploaded product had `colour = null`.
  - New `resolveColour()` helper handles all three shapes the taxonomy uses: `colors` multiselect (`string[]` → first element), `colors` free text (`"20+ colors"` → as-is), and `color` single select. Written on both insert and update; `ProductPatch`/`EditableProduct` gained `colour`; edit mode prefills `formValues.colors = [row.colour]`.
  - `DynamicFormFields` multiselect renders a truncation note when `field.id === "colors"` and more than one is selected: "Only the first colour (X) is saved on the listing. Multiple colours per product are not supported yet." The Pricing step also shows a read-only `Colour` line, matching the sizes summary.

Test rows deleted (`products` back to 29). `tsc --noEmit --skipLibCheck` 0 errors, eslint clean, `vite build` passes.
  - **PRE-EXISTING BUG FOUND, NOT FIXED — vendor product edits are silently discarded.** `Upload.tsx` handleSubmit opens with `if (category?.type !== "product") { toast.success(...); return; }`. In edit mode the category/sub-category steps are skipped so `selectedCategory` is never set, `category` is null, and the guard trips: the handler returns before `updateProduct()`, shows a misleading **"Profile submitted! Your listing is now pending review."** toast, and issues **no PATCH at all** (confirmed by network trace: only the GET fires). This predates Stage 0/1 entirely. Consequence: the `sizes`/`colour`/`customization_available` additions to the update path are unreachable until it is fixed. - 2026-07-26: **Razorpay go-live for subscriptions + ads — config, deploy tooling, and three fixes on the live-money path.** `supabase/config.toml` (new), `scripts/setup-razorpay.ps1` (new), `supabase/RAZORPAY.md` (new), `supabase/functions/razorpay-create-order/index.ts`, `supabase/functions/subscription-create-order/index.ts`, `src/lib/queries/payments.ts`, `src/lib/queries/subscriptions.ts`. The six edge functions and both client flows were **already fully implemented** — no new payment logic was written. What was missing was configuration, plus three defects that only bite once real keys are set.
  - **`supabase/config.toml` did not exist**, so `functions deploy` would have shipped all six functions with the CLI default `verify_jwt = true`. Correct for the four the browser calls, **fatal for the two webhooks**: Razorpay posts server-to-server with only an `x-razorpay-signature` header, so the platform would have 401'd every callback *before* the handler ran and the "browser closed before verify-payment" backstop would have silently never fired — the failure mode is invisible until a vendor pays and gets nothing. Now pins `verify_jwt = false` on `razorpay-webhook` + `subscription-webhook` and `true` on the other four. The webhooks are not unauthenticated; they authenticate the payload via HMAC-SHA256 of the raw body and 400 on mismatch.
  - **Fix 1 — unchecked payment intent (both create-order functions).** The `ad_orders` / `subscription_payment_orders` insert result was discarded, and in the ad function the whole write was wrapped in `if (url && serviceKey)`. If that insert failed the function still returned the order and the client still opened Checkout, so money was collected against an intent that did not exist. Fulfilment then hit `publishOrder` / `activateFromOrder`'s "already paid / unknown" branch, which returns `ok: true` with `count: 0` — **the vendor is charged, receives no campaign or plan, and the UI reports success.** Both now check the insert and return `intent_failed` before Checkout opens; the env lookup is hoisted above the Razorpay call so it bails before creating anything. Cost of failing there is one unpaid orphan order at Razorpay, which expires on its own.
  - **Fix 2 — error responses fell through to the *demo* branch (both client helpers).** `createRazorpayOrder` tested `data.error === "not_configured" || !data.configured` in one condition, making the following `if (data.error) throw` unreachable for every other error. So `order_failed` (e.g. rejected credentials) and the new `intent_failed` returned `{configured: false}` → the caller ran the simulated checkout → **ads published and plans activated for free, with no charge.** `createSubscriptionOrder` had the identical shape. Reordered so only `not_configured` may simulate; every other error now throws and surfaces as the existing "Checkout failed" toast. This was latent before today only because no keys were set, so `not_configured` was the sole error anyone ever hit.
  - **Setup tooling.** `scripts/setup-razorpay.ps1` checks CLI auth, prompts for the key secret and webhook secret as `SecureString` (never echoed, never written to disk, never committed — the key *ID* is not secret since it ships to the browser in Checkout), sets the three function secrets, and deploys the six functions one at a time so a failure names the function. Idempotent. `supabase/RAZORPAY.md` documents the architecture, the two-webhook registration, verification, and rollback (`secrets unset RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET` reverts both flows to simulated checkout with no redeploy, since every function branches on the secret's presence).
  - **Note on the shared account:** both webhook URLs receive every `payment.captured` for the account and each looks up the order id in its own intent table only, so an ad payment no-ops in `subscription-webhook` and vice versa. Intended, not a misconfiguration. Fulfilment is idempotent across verify-payment and the webhook via a conditional `'created' → 'paid'` UPDATE — whichever lands first wins — which is what makes double-fulfilment impossible.
  - **Verified:** `npm run build` clean; `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json` holds at the pre-existing baseline with **no errors in either touched client file** (remaining errors are all in `src/lib/i18n.ts`, `src/lib/queries/profile.ts`, `src/lib/queries/vendorDashboard.ts`); `config.toml` parses (the CLI advances past it to the auth check); the PowerShell script parses via `[Parser]::ParseFile`; `secrets set` / `secrets unset` flag shapes confirmed against CLI 2.109.1. **Not executed:** secrets are not set and nothing is deployed — the CLI holds no access token, so `npx supabase login` is required first. The two Razorpay-dashboard webhooks are also still unregistered. Edge functions were not typechecked (no local Deno).
  - **Flagged, not changed:** the key supplied is `rzp_live_*`, so the first successful checkout moves real money; `ad_orders.status = 'refund_review'` (a Free-plan vendor who paid for ads) has **no automated refund** and needs a manual dashboard refund; `ad_orders` is `ALTER`ed by `20260719122000_...sql` but never `CREATE`d in any migration — it exists in the live DB and in `database.types.ts`, so it was created out-of-band and a fresh environment would not reproduce it.

Each card: color-coded type avatar, title with unread dot, body, per-type CTA (requirement → blue "Quote now" → `/leads`; others → outline actions → `/quotes`, `/chat`, `/advertisements`, `/reviews`, `/business-profile-score`, `/my-store`), relative time (`date-fns`), hover-dismiss (X), and an unread left-rail + blue tint. Requirement cards render meta chips (category · location · quantity · budget · deadline). Empty state per tab. Opening a card marks it read.
  - **`src/App.tsx`** — imported `Notifications`, added `<Route path="/notifications" … />`.
  - **Design-skill polish pass (`design-taste-frontend`).** Reworked the page against the taste-skill craft rules (the skill is landing-page oriented but its bias-correction rules apply): (1) **Color-consistency lock** — replaced the 8-hue "one colour per type" avatar palette (indigo/sky/violet/green/amber/emerald/slate…) with a restrained **4-tone, meaning-first system** dominated by brand blue: `brand` (blue = a new opportunity: requirements + leads), `positive` (emerald = money won: quote accepted, payment), `warning` (amber = needs attention: ad budget low), `neutral` (slate = informational). Added a `tone` field to `VendorNotification`; icon is still chosen by `type`, colour by `tone`. (2) **Decorative-dot ban** — the unread state was signalled three ways (tint + left rail + inline dot); now it's a single subtle brand tint + **one** small leading dot, read rows are plain white. (3) **Em-dash ban** — audited every user-visible string; changed "Payment received — ₹1,95,000" to "Payment received: ₹1,95,000" (remaining `—` are only in `//` code comments, stripped at build). (4) **Tactile feedback / shape lock / motivated motion** — `whileTap` + `active:translate-y-px` on buttons, one radius scale (containers `rounded-2xl`, icon tiles `rounded-xl`, pills/buttons `rounded-full`), entry stagger only. Also added: a **requirements spotlight** banner (shown only when unread requirements > 0, taps through to the Requirements tab — reinforces the headline "new buyer requirements" use-case), a **sticky filter bar** (`top-14 lg:top-16`), a **stretched-link card** pattern (full-card click target with `pointer-events` re-enabled on the CTA / mark-read / dismiss controls so nested interactives stay valid), and a per-card **mark-as-read** (check) affordance on hover. Standardized lucide `strokeWidth`. `markUnread` added to the store for completeness.
  - **Verified:** `tsc` clean for all changed files (remaining project TS errors are pre-existing/unrelated); `npm run build` clean (only the pre-existing bundle-size warning); Playwright screenshots at 1440px and 390px confirm the spotlight, sticky tabs, tone-coloured cards, unread tint+dot, requirement chips, time grouping (Today / Earlier this week), and per-type CTAs all render correctly on desktop + mobile.

- 2026-07-25: **Buyer brand page (`/vendor/:id`) — Brand Product Videos is now a horizontal slider (was a grid), incl. a mouse-drag fix.** `src/pages/VendorProfile.tsx`. Converted the `grid grid-cols-3 lg:grid-cols-6` into a drag-scroll rail (`flex gap-3 overflow-x-auto scrollbar-hide`) of larger single-row tiles (`w-32 lg:w-40 shrink-0`) using the shared `useDragScroll` hook. The hook's `onClickCapture` swallows the post-drag click so tapping a thumbnail still opens `VideoCloseUpsViewer` while a mouse-drag only pans.
  - **Mouse-drag bug + real root cause.** First cut felt dead to a **mouse** (touch/trackpad still scrolled). Diagnosed empirically with Playwright, not guessed: a differential drag-from-tile test passed on the known-good New Arrivals reel rail (0→25) but failed on this one (0→0), and instrumenting the rail showed the events *did* reach it (`mousedown:1, mousemove:5, dragstart:0`) yet `scrollLeft` never moved. Cause was **scroll-snap** (`snap-x` on the rail + `snap-start` on tiles) fighting the JS `scrollLeft` drag — the `useDragScroll` hook's `scrollSnapType="none"`-during-drag guard wasn't enough here. Removing scroll-snap (matching the working reel rail, which has none) fixed it: drag-from-tile now 0→75. Also switched the tiles from `motion.button` (framer `whileTap`) to plain `<button>` and set the img `draggable={false}` + `pointer-events-none`, mirroring the proven rail. Verified via Playwright: drag pans (0→75) **and** a plain click still opens the viewer (`<video>` + fullscreen overlay render). `npm run build` clean.

Lighter card border (`gray-200 → gray-100`), smaller portrait thumbnails (`aspect-[3/4]`, `w-[4.75rem] lg:w-24`), more whitespace. Dropped the now-unused `MessageCircle` import (added `Heart`).   - **Back bug fix.** The header back button was hardcoded `navigate("/profile/help")`, so coming from Profile → "Chat with Us" and pressing Back landed on the Help Center instead of Profile. Replaced with a smart `goBack()` that uses React Router's history index (`window.history.state.idx`): `idx > 0 → navigate(-1)` (returns to wherever the user came from — Profile), else `navigate("/profile")` as a deep-link/refresh fallback. Verified via Playwright: Profile → chat → Back lands on `/profile`. (The route is only reached from Profile's "Chat with Us" row; the Help page uses its own inline `ChatModal`, not this route.)

Net result: **mobile sees a card every 10 products (every 5 mobile rows: 10, 20, 30…); desktop sees one every 20 products (every 5 desktop rows: 20, 40…)**. Never trails the final product (`n >= products.length` guard). Wired into the two real product feeds on the page — "Following New-In" and "Most Popular"; "Following Top Performing" (fixed 4-item highlight) is left alone.

  - **`FollowingViewAll.tsx`** — added `follow` to its `useFollowing()` destructure, added a `discoverable` memo (`brands.filter(b => !b.isHidden)`, same reasoning as the main Following page: includes already-followed brands so a just-followed slide can show "Following" instead of vanishing), and rendered `<NewBrandsCarousel brands={discoverable} onFollow={(b) => follow(b.id)} />` at the very top of the page content, above the search bar and the followed-brands list. Page's existing `max-w-2xl` (no desktop-specific width) left as-is — not asked to widen it for desktop.

- 2026-07-20/21: **Followings page (buyer) — Follow→Following toggle fix, plus "Your Followings" 2-column desktop layout (briefly reverted on a false alarm, then restored).** `src/pages/Following.tsx`. Verified via build + Playwright (1280px desktop, 390px mobile, plus an actual click-through of the Follow button) at each step.
  - **Fixed: clicking Follow on "Looking for New Brands?" made the card vanish instead of showing "Following."** The carousel's `brands` prop was the parent's `suggested` list — filtered to exclude anyone already followed — so the instant a follow succeeded, the next render dropped that brand from the array and the slide disappeared before the button could ever show a "Following" state. Fixed two ways together: (1) parent now passes a broader `discoverable` list (`brands.filter(b => !b.isHidden)`, i.e. includes already-followed brands) instead of the followed-excluding `suggested`; (2) `NewBrandsCarousel` now freezes which brand ids get a slide the first time each is seen not-yet-followed (`seenRef`/`slideIds`, only ever grows), and each slide's Follow/Following button reads the *live* `isFollowing` off that id — so following a brand flips its own button (check icon, muted style, "Following" label, no-op on click) without the slide leaving the carousel.
  - **"Your Followings" 2-column desktop split (currently in place).** `followed.length > 3` gates a `lg:hidden` single column (unchanged mobile/small-desktop behavior) vs. a `hidden lg:grid lg:grid-cols-2 lg:divide-x lg:divide-gray-100` two-column split (`slice(0,3)` / `slice(3,6)`, up to 6 total). Does not duplicate real brands to force a full 6 when there are fewer than 4 — stays single-column in that case.
  - **Note on the brief revert:** this was pulled out once already after the user reported "the entire section removed," which read at the time like a complaint about this layout change. It turned out to be an unrelated stale-dev-server/browser-cache issue (confirmed via `git status`: nothing from that session had been committed, and a fresh `tsc`/build were clean) — the user then explicitly asked to re-add the 2-column layout. Restored as originally implemented.
